    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/variants/execution-denied.response.md",
      "bytes": 557,
      "sha256": "81e80cea060e4dd06849059d964a42f0684339f7258331270516d857df7f8617",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "Execution is unavailable in this variant. The formatter source is readable, but there is no executable pre-change behavior pin. Structural movement stops here. Proposed work is to capture literal toFixed(2) behavior for 0, 1.005, -1.005, 12.345, empty rows, NaN and Infinity; remove fixed and lastFormatted; migrate formatInvoice and textual references to formatAmount; then replay the pin. No fixture implementation was changed or run in this restricted branch. The independent normal-branch observations do not supply execution evidence for this variant.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/variants/no-workers/work/exporter.mjs",
      "bytes": 190,
      "sha256": "22018eca9aab2ac09a5e9319751d5c6d9a171b30812748a274d5c331d8c6b392",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { formatRows } from './formatter.mjs';\nexport function exportAmounts(rows) { return formatRows(rows).join('\\n'); }\nexport const publicFixtureSymbols = ['formatAmount', 'formatRows'];\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/variants/no-workers/work/decision.md",
      "bytes": 1191,
      "sha256": "4342ec876e76fc561059a18f43c6b8f8da27743736a4e7003f48c194b10c86c3",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Synthetic invoice decision record\n\nDisplay contract: JavaScript Number.toFixed(2), including its binary floating-point rounding behavior. Do not silently replace this with accounting decimal rules. Empty row lists produce []. Nonfinite amounts throw Error('nonfinite'). Capture observed outputs before edits for 0, 1.005, -1.005, 12.345, empty rows, NaN and Infinity. Output examples in a test are expectations, not proof a test ran.\n\nFixture history decision: a UI consumer compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports formatRows. This is why rounding belongs at the display boundary. No customer statement or external ticket was retrieved. Errors are thrown by formatAmount and are not swallowed by the controller. Alternative A removes fixed and lastFormatted; alternative B introduces a stateful Formatter class. Compare reader cost and behavioral equivalence.\n\nAuthorized internal rename target for preserved-behavior: formatAmount -> formatAmount. Migrate imports, docs and string references together. sortAmounts is a known unrelated defect and must remain separate. Calculator cache is observable through inspectCachedTotal; changing it requires checking that consumer.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/variants/no-workers/work/formatter.mjs",
      "bytes": 369,
      "sha256": "bf0206ae81c99c2761d7673c1508bf33d8845f158cd10198bf3908e851017089",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "export function formatAmount(value) {\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\n  return value.toFixed(2);\n}\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatAmount(row.total)); }\n// Separate known bug, outside formatter refactor: this sorts numeric strings lexically.\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/variants/no-workers/pin.mjs",
      "bytes": 1135,
      "sha256": "b493349b7d1eda88fed3bd5f4e5385e1205e713666638566ca3d838d447bf93f",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { writeFileSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\nconst root='/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/variants/no-workers';\nconst module=await import(`${root}/work/formatter.mjs`);\nconst {exportAmounts,publicFixtureSymbols}=await import(`${root}/work/exporter.mjs`);\nconst format=module.formatAmount??module.formatInvoice;\nconst observations=[];\nfor(const value of [0,1.005,-1.005,12.345,NaN,Infinity]) {try{observations.push({input:String(value),value:format(value)});}catch(error){observations.push({input:String(value),error:{name:error.name,message:error.message}});}}\nobservations.push({emptyRows:module.formatRows([]),rows:module.formatRows([{total:1.005},{total:12.345}]),csv:exportAmounts([{total:0},{total:-1.005}]),knownUnrelatedSort:module.sortAmounts(['10','2'])});\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(observations,null,2));\nif(process.argv[2]==='baseline')writeFileSync(`${root}/baseline-observations.json`,JSON.stringify(observations,null,2));\nelse if(JSON.stringify(observations)!==JSON.stringify(JSON.parse(readFileSync(`${root}/baseline-observations.json`,'utf8'))))throw new Error('Behavior changed');\n"
    },
    {

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "[\n  {\n    \"input\": \"0\",\n    \"value\": \"0.00\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"input\": \"1.005\",\n    \"value\": \"1.00\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"input\": \"-1.005\",\n    \"value\": \"-1.00\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"input\": \"12.345\",\n    \"value\": \"12.35\"\n  },\n  {\n    \"input\": \"NaN\",\n    \"error\": {\n      \"name\": \"Error\",\n      \"message\": \"nonfinite\"\n    }\n  },\n  {\n    \"input\": \"Infinity\",\n    \"error\": {\n      \"name\": \"Error\",\n      \"message\": \"nonfinite\"\n    }\n  },\n  {\n    \"emptyRows\": [],\n    \"rows\": [\n      \"1.00\",\n      \"12.35\"\n    ],\n    \"csv\": \"0.00\\n-1.00\",\n    \"knownUnrelatedSort\": [\n      \"10\",\n      \"2\"\n    ]\n  }\n]"
    },

      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/candidate-B.mjs",
      "bytes": 462,

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "class Formatter {\n  lastFormatted = '';\n  format(value) {\n    if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\n    this.lastFormatted = value.toFixed(2);\n    return this.lastFormatted;\n  }\n}\nconst formatter = new Formatter();\nexport function formatInvoice(value) { return formatter.format(value); }\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatInvoice(row.total)); }\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/tool-003.json",

      "sha256": "d7609ec9eb412a24ab6cc55e4ff27b876013d01fec49e9a5eb3b366da7c7df81",

      "text": "{\"request\":{\"cmd\":\"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/refactoring.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/host-capabilities.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/model-roles.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/orchestration-contract.md; cat /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/work/*; cat /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/pin.mjs\",\"max_output_tokens\":22000},\"raw\":{\"chunk_id\":\"0fd43b\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000047201,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":6265,\"output\":\"### Refactoring\\n\\nRead the complete [mode](../SKILL.md) and each applied principle leaf before acting. Copy all eight steps verbatim into the available todo list, or an authorized local checklist with a disclosed reduced binding, before task-specific items. Discover [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), follow [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Missing required reads or operations stop dependent work. Preserve the mode's companion, comments and matching-control gates.\\n\\n**You own the contract. The structure changes. The behavior does not.** Distinct from Feature, which adds behavior, and Bug fix, which corrects it.\\n\\nIf the cleanup reveals a missing feature or a real bug, split it out and ship the structural change first against the pinned contract. A redesign is allowed, but name it and route to Feature. Large or cross-cutting structural work belongs to the [figure-it-out](../../figure-it-out/SKILL.md) skill. This playbook is the focused-to-medium change.\\n\\n1. Pin the behavior contract first. Run the [how](../../how/SKILL.md) skill over the affected subsystem to learn the contract, then write a characterization test, snapshot, or equivalence harness that captures current behavior before any structure moves. If the area has no coverage, write the pin before touching structure. Type check and lint are not a pin.\\n2. Name the structure the code is missing per [principle-model-the-domain](../../principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md). Boring code stays when the shape is already clear and local. The reshape must delete branches or invalid states, not add indirection.\\n3. Name the target shape. State what the module layout, types, and call graph should be if built today ([principle-foundational-thinking](../../principle-foundational-thinking/SKILL.md), [principle-redesign-from-first-principles](../../principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md)). If the target crosses a function boundary, run the [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) skill for parallel design exploration of the shape before the move.\\n4. Subtract before you add. Delete dead code, collapse one-caller wrappers, drop redundant validators, and remove orphan references before introducing the new shape ([principle-subtract-before-you-add](../../principle-subtract-before-you-add/SKILL.md)). The smallest change that reaches the target shape ships ([principle-laziness-protocol](../../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md)). A speculative cleanup that \\\"might help\\\" gets reverted.\\n5. Move in small behavior-preserving steps, each keeping the pin green. For API reshapes, migrate every caller and delete the old API in the same wave ([principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis](../../principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis/SKILL.md)). No compatibility shims, no parallel old-and-new paths. Spot-check every rename against the actual files. Renames silently miss usages in strings, prose, and back-references. Delegate the mechanical edits to an authorized independent worker with a specific scope, including file paths, the names being moved and the behavior to hold. Supply the complete [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and record the exact input revision, owned output paths, worker identity and inspected artifacts. Use inherited model and effort unless a supported configured refactoring selection is observed. Record requested and actual selections separately; unknown actual models prove no diversity. Review the returned diff yourself. When workers are unavailable or unauthorized, perform authorized mechanical edits sequentially only as reduced capability and leave the independent delegation gate unsatisfied.\\n6. Prove behavior is unchanged on the real artifact, not \\\"it compiles\\\" ([principle-prove-it-works](../../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md)). For larger reshapes, run an equivalence check: a script that diffs old-vs-new outputs, a recorded baseline replayed against the new code, or a smoke run on the matching surface via the relevant control skill.\\n7. Confirm the change is worth keeping. The success measure is reduced reader load ([principle-minimize-reader-load](../../principle-minimize-reader-load/SKILL.md)). If the diff does not lower reader load somewhere, revert it.\\n8. Rebase into small ordered commits. A subtraction commit, then the reshape, then any follow-on cleanup. Shape them with the [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md) principle skill, so each behavior-preserving slice stays green before the next. Rebase and commit only with authorization and an available operation, preserving concurrent work. Otherwise return the checked local diff and ordered delivery plan with these operations unperformed. Run **Opening a PR** by reading `skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/opening-a-pr.md` in full and following its complete contract when available and authorized. This is a deferred task 21 dependency for task 24 integration until that file exists; stop PR-dependent work if absent. No push or PR creation follows from this playbook without explicit authorization. Opening a PR never starts Babysit implicitly.\\n\\n**Reply:** the structure that changed, the pin you held it against, the equivalence proof, the reader-load delta, what shipped and what got reverted. No new behavior.\\n\\nIf writes or execution are unavailable, return a source-grounded proposal and identify the unperformed pin, edits or equivalence checks. Missing behavior evidence blocks unchanged-behavior claims. Structural validation, type checks, lint and a worker self-report never prove equivalence. Report each missing capability and denied gate alongside the partial artifacts.\\n# Discover capabilities in the current session\\n\\nRead the tools exposed to this session before assigning work. Cached official-subagents.txt is documentation context; it proves no operation succeeded. Local agent configuration is not portable plugin configuration.\\n\\nRecord each binding as `{operation, tool, exposed, succeeded, evidence, limits}`. `tool` is the exact exposed identifier or null when absent. `succeeded` is true only after observing a result. Keep failed and unavailable bindings. Successful bindings become `successfulOperations` in host evidence, with a root-relative evidence file. The operation labels below describe purposes, never callable APIs.\\n\\n| Operation | Discovery and observation required |\\n| --- | --- |\\n| spawn | Find an exposed independent-worker operation; retain returned worker identity and exact assigned revision. |\\n| follow-up | Find the operation that sends input to that worker; observe acceptance by the same identity. |\\n| wait | Find the operation that waits for worker state; retain terminal or interrupted state. |\\n| inspect-result | Read the worker result and its referenced artifacts; a wait notification alone is insufficient. |\\n| stop | Observe cancellation or termination through the exposed operation. |\\n| read | Observe the requested file content through the available file tool. |\\n| isolated-write | Observe a write within the worker's assigned path and permissions. |\\n| history | Observe available prior turns or records; declare gaps explicitly. |\\n| execution | Observe command execution with exit status and output. |\\n| persistence | Write and read an authorized durable record; a claimed save is insufficient. |\\n| external-write | Record explicit authorization and action identity; inspect the result before replay. |\\n| resolve-ownership | Observe distinct owned paths before contested writes. |\\n| inspect-terminal | Observe the worker's terminal state after stop. |\\n| quarantine-artifacts | Observe partial artifacts withheld from integration. |\\n| inspect-prior-action | Read the interrupted action identity and prior outcome. |\\n| inspect-destination | Observe the destination state for that action identity. |\\n| safe-recovery | Observe explicitly authorized idempotent recovery after identity and destination inspection. |\\n\\nNo default API names are assigned here. Tool names differ by host and session. An exposed tool may still be denied or fail. Record requested and actual models only when observed; otherwise use `unknown`. A requested model never proves the actual model.\\n\\nA denied or unavailable operation blocks the branch that requires it. Sequential substitution for required independent work is reduced capability and uses `reduced-pass`; ordinary direct completion can fully pass. Missing persistence limits session pickup even when current work succeeds. Preserve user authorization and host permission boundaries through every fallback.\\n\\nWorker passes require observed spawn and result inspection. Each registry entry declares `requiredObservations` for `pass` and `reduced-pass`; tool observations need exposed bindings and existing evidence artifacts; native text observations use the separate reviewed native record below. Full cancellation requires stop, terminal inspection and artifact quarantine. Full persistence requires durable write and readback. Full interrupted-action recovery requires prior identity inspection, destination inspection, safe recovery, explicit external permission and a recorded prior action identity even when no external-write observation is listed.\\n\\nPermission checks follow performed operations. Isolated writes and persistence require granted writes. External writes and safe recovery require granted external permission and prior identity. Authorized read-only reductions may retain denied writes; their limits must preserve denied or unavailable permissions. Required observations remain applicable to those reductions.\\n\\nHost evidence is stored as object or array JSON files under `docs/parity/evidence/`. Required fields are defined by `docs/parity/evidence.schema.json`. Artifacts and operation evidence must be existing files inside the tested root. Test fixtures belong in `tests/` and prove validator behavior only.\\n\\nRun `python3 tools/validate_evidence.py --root . --commit <tested-package-commit>` through Bun, for example `bun -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\\\"python3\\\",\\\"-B\\\",\\\"tools/validate_evidence.py\\\",\\\"--root\\\",\\\".\\\",\\\"--commit\\\",process.argv[1]],{env:{...process.env,PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE:\\\"1\\\"}});process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' <tested-package-commit>`. It reads every JSON record below the evidence directory, prints each error, and exits 1 for invalid records or 0 for valid records. An empty directory has no invalid records and supplies no behavioral evidence.\\n\\nThe required registry file in `docs/parity/case-registry.json` declares applicability. Missing or non-file registry, unknown case, structural-only entry or unlisted host rejects behavioral validation. Maintenance fixtures use a temporary copy of this registry; they cannot bypass applicability. Required hosts must receive observed evidence or explicit blocked records before release claims. Structural checks have no required host and cannot populate host status. Later producers propose task-owned entries; only the coordinator integrates them. Source paths select callers through the task 3 case contract. Case definitions alone leave host statuses `not-run`.\\n\\n## Direct completion and recorded observations\\n\\n`direct` means the task is completed in the current context. Restatement, supplied-text writing and complete installed discovery can pass directly. `sequential` is reserved for replacing a required independent method with sequential work and remains reduced. A text response has no invented callable tool. Record supplied input, the actual response and qualitative outcome review as `nativeObservations`, each with its existing listed evidence artifact and named reviewer. Their labels are `supplied-input`, `response` and `review-outcome`. The reviewer assesses the case's expected outcome; validation checks evidence relationships and does not decide prose quality.\\n\\nEach registry entry declares allowed execution modes and required permissions for each status. A grant is required for the performed action: worker spawn, command execution and stop need execution; isolated output or persistence needs writes; external actions need their separate permission and prior identity. Read-only discovery does not inherit an execution grant requirement. Tool observations still name an actually exposed tool and existing evidence. Native observations cannot claim tool discovery, worker identity or saved writes.\\n\\nFull Arena and Swarm results additionally provide `inputRevision` and a listed `methodReceipt` artifact. An exposed `inspect-method` operation must reference that receipt. Assignment operations name `assignmentId` and the corresponding inspected output. See the [structured method contract](orchestration-contract.md). All positive maintenance fixtures are synthetic; none is an observed host result.\\n# Model selection for portable roles\\n\\nUse [session capabilities](host-capabilities.md) before assigning a role. These [poteto](agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and [comment reviewer](agent-prompts/comment-sicko.md) prompts are instructions supplied to an exposed worker operation. Reading them does not register a custom agent or authorize delegation.\\n\\n| Role | Default requested model | Observed model | Supported effort | Fallback disclosure |\\n| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |\\n| poteto-agent | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n| comment-sicko | inherit-parent | unknown until observed | inherit-parent unless a supported explicit effort is verified | Record rejected selection, replacement request, observed result and limits. |\\n\\nFor each assignment, record requested model and effort, observed model and effort, supported effort values and their source, worker identity, and fallback disclosure. Use `unknown` for information the operation does not expose. `inherit-parent` records a selection policy, not an observed model name. Do not silently convert a requested model into an actual model.\\n\\nSelect an explicit model only from current exposed tool documentation or observed enumeration results. If enumeration is missing, disclose that limit and retain `inherit-parent`. Documentation examples are not a list of available models. Use only an effort supported for the selected model by the current operation; do not carry an incompatible inherited effort into an explicit selection.\\n\\nIf a selected model or effort is rejected, retain the rejection. Use an authorized inherited or supported alternative only after disclosing the changed selection. If the task requires that exact selection, stop and record blocked. A fallback never proves the original selection succeeded.\\n\\nIdentical observed models mean no model diversity. Different roles can supply distinct instructions on the same model. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified, even when requested model names differ. Different observed models support a model-diversity statement only for those observed assignments. Worker independence still requires successful spawn and result inspection.\\n\\nWhen workers are unavailable, apply an authorized role sequentially and label reduced capability. Preserve scope and permission boundaries. Do not claim independent review, background execution, or full worker support from sequential role switching.\\n\\nThe cached documentation from https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-configuration/subagents describes inheritance and optional local agent configuration. It is context only. This package writes no local custom-agent configuration and claims no current host model or worker support from that documentation.\\n# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n# Synthetic invoice decision record\\n\\nDisplay contract: JavaScript Number.toFixed(2), including its binary floating-point rounding behavior. Do not silently replace this with accounting decimal rules. Empty row lists produce []. Nonfinite amounts throw Error('nonfinite'). Capture observed outputs before edits for 0, 1.005, -1.005, 12.345, empty rows, NaN and Infinity. Output examples in a test are expectations, not proof a test ran.\\n\\nFixture history decision: a UI consumer compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports formatRows. This is why rounding belongs at the display boundary. No customer statement or external ticket was retrieved. Errors are thrown by formatInvoice and are not swallowed by the controller. Alternative A removes fixed and lastFormatted; alternative B introduces a stateful Formatter class. Compare reader cost and behavioral equivalence.\\n\\nAuthorized internal rename target for preserved-behavior: formatInvoice -> formatAmount. Migrate imports, docs and string references together. sortAmounts is a known unrelated defect and must remain separate. Calculator cache is observable through inspectCachedTotal; changing it requires checking that consumer.\\nimport { formatRows } from './formatter.mjs';\\nexport function exportAmounts(rows) { return formatRows(rows).join('\\\\n'); }\\nexport const publicFixtureSymbols = ['formatInvoice', 'formatRows'];\\nlet lastFormatted = '';\\nfunction fixed(value) { return value.toFixed(2); }\\nexport function formatInvoice(value) {\\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\\n  lastFormatted = fixed(value);\\n  return lastFormatted;\\n}\\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatInvoice(row.total)); }\\n// Separate known bug, outside formatter refactor: this sorts numeric strings lexically.\\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\\nimport { writeFileSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\\nconst root='/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior';\\nconst module=await import(`${root}/work/formatter.mjs`);\\nconst {exportAmounts,publicFixtureSymbols}=await import(`${root}/work/exporter.mjs`);\\nconst format=module.formatAmount??module.formatInvoice;\\nconst observations=[];\\nfor(const value of [0,1.005,-1.005,12.345,NaN,Infinity]) {try{observations.push({input:String(value),value:format(value)});}catch(error){observations.push({input:String(value),error:{name:error.name,message:error.message}});}}\\nobservations.push({emptyRows:module.formatRows([]),rows:module.formatRows([{total:1.005},{total:12.345}]),csv:exportAmounts([{total:0},{total:-1.005}]),knownUnrelatedSort:module.sortAmounts(['10','2'])});\\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(observations,null,2));\\nif(process.argv[2]==='baseline')writeFileSync(`${root}/baseline-observations.json`,JSON.stringify(observations,null,2));\\nelse if(JSON.stringify(observations)!==JSON.stringify(JSON.parse(readFileSync(`${root}/baseline-observations.json`,'utf8'))))throw new Error('Behavior changed');\\n\"}}\n"

Do not silently replace this with accounting decimal rules. Empty row lists produce []. Nonfinite amounts throw Error('nonfinite'). Capture observed outputs before edits for 0, 1.005, -1.005, 12.345, empty rows, NaN and Infinity. Output examples in a test are expectations, not proof a test ran.\\\\n \\\\n-Fixture history decision: a UI consumer compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports formatRows. Calculator cache is observable through inspectCachedTotal; changing it requires checking that consumer.\\\\n\\\",\\\"stderr\\\":\\\"\\\"}\\n\\nCandidate A keeps the finite-number boundary and literal toFixed call in a pure function. No parallel arena, independent judge, model diversity, or full architect completion is claimed.\\n\\n\"}}\n"

      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/tool-005.json",
      "bytes": 18030,
      "sha256": "513e083fe3ab31261b1997fcc473e07bf8a10ccdccada30edb19d7f924fa8b04",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\"request\":{\"cmd\":\"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/technical-writing/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/how/references/explainer-prompt.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/architect/references/design-red-flags.md; git -C /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/task-input rev-parse HEAD\",\"max_output_tokens\":10000},\"raw\":{\"chunk_id\":\"1a3b9e\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000033583,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":4263,\"output\":\"---\\nname: technical-writing\\ndescription: \\\"Layered technical-writing standard: Diátaxis structure, Google developer style sentences, STE instruction rules, Global English syntax. Use for /technical-writing or when writing or reviewing docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Technical writing\\n\\nThe goal is writing a tired engineer understands on the first read. Four layers get you there, one question each: what kind of document is this, how do sentences address the reader, how much does each sentence carry, and can any sentence be read two ways. Apply all four.\\n\\nThree rules sit above the layers:\\n\\n- **Cut every word that does no work.** If the sentence survives without a word, the word goes. \\\"In order to\\\" is \\\"to\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" is nothing.\\n- **Use the short, everyday word.** \\\"Use\\\", not \\\"utilize\\\". \\\"Help\\\", not \\\"facilitate\\\". \\\"Do\\\", not \\\"perform\\\". A long word has to buy its length with precision.\\n- **When a rule makes a sentence worse, fix the sentence another way or leave it alone.** The rules serve the reader. A sentence that follows every rule and sounds like a machine wrote it has failed.\\n\\nThe codebase is the word list. Write the real symbol, file, flag, or command name, not a synonym or a description of it.\\n\\nDon't invent jargon. Use the words a developer would say out loud: \\\"move\\\", \\\"delete\\\", \\\"a budget that only decreases\\\", not \\\"evacuate\\\", \\\"ratchet\\\", or \\\"endgame\\\". A named pattern is fine when the doc says what it means the first time. Propose a new offender and its replacement as an addition to `unslop`'s abstract-metaphor rule in your reply, with the diff. Don't edit that skill.\\n\\n## Vary the rhythm\\n\\nThe layers decide what a document says and how much each sentence carries. A doc can obey all of them and still read machine-written: every sentence clipped short, no view anywhere, nothing specific.\\n\\n- Mix sentence lengths on purpose. Short sentences land a point. Longer ones that take their time carry a fact with its condition or consequence.\\n- One thought per sentence does not mean one length per sentence. Split the sentence that carries two thoughts. Keep the long sentence that carries one.\\n- Have a view where the mode allows it. Explanation weighs trade-offs, so say what you make of them instead of listing pros and cons. Reference stays dry.\\n- Be specific over sterile. Not \\\"schema changes can cause issues\\\" but \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\".\\n\\n## Pick the mode first (Diátaxis)\\n\\nOne document, one mode. Two questions pick it: does the content inform action (doing) or understanding (thinking), and does it serve learning or work?\\n\\n- Action + learning: **tutorial**.\\n- Action + work: **how-to**.\\n- Understanding + work: **reference**.\\n- Understanding + learning: **explanation**.\\n\\nUse the compass on a whole document or on one sentence.\\n\\n**Tutorial: learning by doing.** You are the teacher. The learner's success is your job, not theirs. Open by saying what the learner will build, not what they will \\\"learn\\\". Every step produces a visible result, early and often. Tell them what they should see: the expected output, the prompt change, the log line. Cut explanation to one clause and a link. Teaching pauses break the lesson. Stay concrete. Write as \\\"we\\\", in commands: \\\"First, do x. Now, do y.\\\"\\n\\n**How-to: steps to a goal.** Solve a problem a person has, not an operation the machine can perform. Assume competence. Skip teaching. Action only: no digressions, no background, no completeness for its own sake. Link those instead. Allow forks and judgment: \\\"If you want x, do y.\\\" Name the guide by the task: \\\"How to calibrate the radar array\\\", not \\\"Radar array calibration\\\".\\n\\n**Reference: facts for lookup.** Describe. Only describe. No instruction, no persuasion, no opinion. Be dry, complete, and sure. State facts, options, limits, and errors with no hedging. Mirror the structure of the thing described, so code and docs can be navigated together. Put material where readers expect it. Generate from code where possible, so it stays true.\\n\\n**Explanation: understanding and why.** One bounded topic, readable away from the product. Each title should tolerate an implicit \\\"About...\\\" in front. Anchor on a real why question. Give context: design decisions, history, constraints, alternatives. Opinion is allowed here and nowhere else.\\n\\nDon't mix modes: no reference tables inside a tutorial, no tutorial hand-holding inside reference, no arguing inside a how-to. Split and link instead.\\n\\n## Write sentences to the reader (Google developer style)\\n\\n- Talk to the reader as \\\"you\\\", in the present tense. \\\"Will\\\" only for things that genuinely happen later.\\n- Say who does what: \\\"the compiler checks\\\", not \\\"is checked\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or beside the point.\\n- Write instructions as commands: \\\"Click Submit.\\\" State facts plainly. Never \\\"should be done\\\".\\n- Put the condition before the instruction: \\\"To delete the document, click Delete.\\\" The reader skips what does not apply.\\n- Put the common case first. Exceptions after.\\n- Sound like a knowledgeable friend. No buzzwords, no figurative language, no \\\"please\\\" in instructions, and never \\\"simply\\\", \\\"easy\\\", or \\\"quickly\\\" in a procedure. If it were simple, the reader would not be here.\\n- Don't pre-announce (\\\"we will soon support...\\\") and don't start consecutive sentences with the same phrase.\\n- Link with words that say where the link goes: the page title or a short description. Never \\\"click here\\\". Prefer a sentence of context on the page over a link off it.\\n- Headings carry the point, not just the topic (\\\"Pick the mode first\\\", not \\\"Modes\\\"). Sentence case. A task heading is a bare verb phrase (\\\"Create an instance\\\"). A concept heading is a noun phrase. One h1 per page, no skipped levels.\\n- Numbered lists for sequences, bullets for everything else. Introduce a list with a complete sentence. Keep items parallel.\\n- Code goes in code font. UI elements go in bold. Use serial commas. Drop \\\"etc.\\\" and say up front that a list is partial.\\n\\n## Make statements load one at a time (STE rules)\\n\\n- One instruction per sentence. One thought per sentence everywhere else.\\n- Split instructions longer than about 20 words and other sentences longer than about 25.\\n- Put the warning or condition before the step it guards: \\\"If hot oil touches your skin, injuries can occur.\\\"\\n- Keep \\\"the\\\" and \\\"a\\\": \\\"Remove backup file\\\" reads two ways. \\\"Remove the backup file\\\" reads one.\\n- Give each word one meaning and one job, then keep it. If \\\"check\\\" means inspect, don't also use it for restrain.\\n- Pick one word per action and stick to it: \\\"start\\\", not \\\"start\\\" here and \\\"initiate\\\" there.\\n- Write procedures as direct commands, never as narration and never in the passive: \\\"Install the component\\\", not \\\"the component must be installed\\\".\\n- Avoid \\\"-ing\\\" words where you can. They take too many grammatical jobs and breed misreadings.\\n\\n## Leave no sentence open to two readings (Global English)\\n\\n- Keep words like \\\"only\\\" and \\\"not\\\" next to the word they change: \\\"only fails on growth\\\" and \\\"fails only on growth\\\" say different things.\\n- Break up long noun strings: \\\"the proto import budget check script\\\" becomes \\\"the script that checks the proto-import budget\\\".\\n- Make every \\\"it\\\", \\\"they\\\", and \\\"this\\\" point at one obvious thing. Repeat the noun when in doubt. Never use \\\"this\\\" or \\\"which\\\" to point at a whole clause.\\n- Don't drop verbs: \\\"Phase 1 moves the converters and Phase 2 the runtime\\\" leaves Phase 2 without one. Give it one.\\n- Keep the small words that show structure. \\\"Ensure that the switch is off\\\" keeps \\\"that\\\" because it makes the sentence parse one way. Never trade clarity for word count.\\n- Repeat the article in a series when it prevents a misread: \\\"the client and the host\\\", not \\\"the client and host\\\", when they are two things.\\n- Say which parts \\\"and\\\" or \\\"or\\\" joins when a sentence can group two ways. \\\"Both...and\\\", \\\"either...or\\\", and \\\"if...then\\\" are free disambiguators.\\n- Use periods, not semicolons. Replace an em dash with a new sentence.\\n- Make text in parentheses a full grammatical unit or its own sentence. Never form plurals with \\\"(s)\\\".\\n- No slashes: write \\\"a, b, or both\\\" instead of \\\"a/b\\\" or \\\"and/or\\\".\\n- Call each thing by one name, everywhere. A doc that says \\\"the gate\\\", \\\"the ratchet\\\", and \\\"the budget check\\\" for one thing teaches three things. Rewording an unchanged sentence between edits costs the same way. Don't churn what didn't change.\\n- Skip idioms, colloquialisms, Latin abbreviations, and metaphors. A non-native reader, a translator, and an agent all parse plain constructions best.\\n\\n## Voice and repo specifics\\n\\n- Apply the **unslop** skill to every doc this skill touches. That skill owns the slop-pattern catalog: AI vocabulary, filler, hedging, formatting tells.\\n- PR descriptions and commit messages are writing too. Every layer except Diátaxis applies to them. A PR body is a briefing that a reviewer can read in under a minute. Do not paste swarm logs, SHA lists, or metric tables. Link them.\\n- Product UI strings are not documentation. Use your product's copy guidelines for those.\\n- Indent code snippets with tabs. Write real paths and real symbols. Make every count or tree claim true at the commit that lands it, and include the command that regenerates it.\\n\\n## Worked example\\n\\nBefore:\\n\\n> Configuration of the proto import ratchet budget script parameters is performed via budget.json. Note that it's important to remember that running with --write, which updates the committed budget to reflect the current count, should only be done when lowering it. If exceeded, CI fails.\\n\\nAfter:\\n\\n> `budget.mjs` reads the committed budget from `budget.json` and counts the files that import protos. If the count exceeds the budget, CI fails. Run `budget.mjs --write` only to lower the budget.\\n\\n## Access and permissions\\n\\nUse the current session capabilities in `../../references/host-capabilities.md` to read documents and verify authorized edits. Supplied text is enough for a rewrite in the conversation. If document writes are unavailable or denied, return the proposed text and state that it was not saved. If the document cannot be read and no text was supplied, report the missing input. Do not invent its content. Read `../unslop/SKILL.md` before applying it. If that dependency is unavailable, report the gap and label the review partial. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` as source metadata; host enforcement needs observed evidence. These limits also apply to PR descriptions and commit messages. Sending, publishing, or creating external changes requires explicit authorization.\\n# Explainer Prompt Template\\n\\nBuild the explainer worker's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are writing an architectural explanation for a senior engineer. For complex questions, explorers have traced different slices and gathered findings, independently when supported or sequentially with an explicit reduced-capability disclosure. For simple questions, omit Explorer Findings and explore the source yourself in this role. Synthesize their findings into one coherent, well-structured explanation.\\n\\n## Original Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## Explorer Findings\\n\\n{EXPLORER_FINDINGS_ALL}\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\nThe explorers each investigated a different angle of the same subsystem. Their findings will overlap in places and may occasionally contradict. Reconcile them. Merge overlapping descriptions, resolve contradictions by checking the code yourself, and combine the separate slices into a unified picture.\\n\\nWrite an explanation a senior engineer unfamiliar with this area could read and walk away with a solid mental model, understanding the architecture well enough to start working in it confidently.\\n\\nYou have read-only access to the codebase to check anything, clarify a detail, or fill a gap. Use available file reading, symbol search and file discovery as needed. When explorer findings are supplied, you should not need to re-explore from scratch. Without them, trace entry points, calls, data, abstractions and boundaries yourself; disclose anything you cannot trace.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nUse this structure, adapted to what makes sense for the question. Not every section is needed for every question.\\n\\n### Overview\\n1-2 paragraphs. What is this thing, what does it do, why does it exist. Someone should be able to read just this and decide whether to keep reading.\\n\\n### Key Concepts\\nThe important types, services, or abstractions needed to follow the rest. Brief definitions, not exhaustive.\\n\\n### How It Works\\nThe core of the explanation, and the longest section. Walk through the flow: what triggers it, what happens step by step, where data goes, what the decision points are.\\n\\nUse prose, not pseudocode. Reference specific files and functions so the reader knows where to look, but don't dump large code blocks unless a snippet is essential to a point.\\n\\nWhen the flow involves multiple components talking to each other, or data transforming through stages, include a diagram. Use mermaid (```mermaid) for structured flows (sequence diagrams, flowcharts, component graphs) or ASCII art for simpler relationships where mermaid would be overkill. Use your judgment. A diagram should clarify, not decorate. If prose covers the flow, skip the diagram.\\n\\n### Where Things Live\\nA brief file/directory map. Just the ones someone would need to start working here.\\n\\n### Gotchas\\nNon-obvious things, surprising behavior, historical context, pitfalls. Skip this section if there's nothing worth calling out.\\n\\n## Communication Style\\n\\n- Use concrete language, not abstractions-about-abstractions\\n- Say \\\"the `UserService` calls `AuthClient.refresh()`\\\" not \\\"the service delegates to the client\\\"\\n- When something is complex, explain why it's complex. Don't just describe the complexity\\n- When something is simple, don't pad it out\\n- If there's a helpful analogy, use it. If there isn't, don't force one\\n- If the explorers flagged open questions or gaps, acknowledge them rather than hiding them\\n# Design red flags\\n\\nScreen every candidate before synthesis. A red flag is a reason to revise or reject the shape.\\n\\n## Shallow module\\n\\nA shallow module exposes a large interface while hiding little complexity. Judge depth by the capability and policy hidden behind the public surface relative to the size of that surface. Prefer a simple interface backed by substantial behavior.\\n\\nDo not confuse a deep module with a deep call chain. A deep call chain scatters understanding across layers. A deep module concentrates capability behind one interface.\\n\\nLook for these signs:\\n\\n- Callers coordinate several methods to complete one operation.\\n- Public options expose internal stages or implementation choices.\\n- Learning the interface does not save the caller from learning the implementation.\\n\\n## Information leakage\\n\\nInformation leakage makes multiple modules depend on the same internal decision. A representation, policy, or protocol detail appears in more than one place, so changing it requires coordinated edits.\\n\\nPublic re-exports of transport or wire types are leakage. Parse external data into domain types behind the interface. Keep storage schemas, framework objects, and protocol details private.\\n\\n## Temporal decomposition\\n\\nTemporal decomposition organizes modules by execution order instead of the knowledge they own. Separate load, validate, transform, and save stages often repeat one representation and its invariants across several boundaries.\\n\\nGroup code around domain knowledge and ownership. Methods that run at different times can still belong to one module when they protect the same decisions.\\n\\n## Pass-through method\\n\\nA pass-through method forwards the same arguments to another method with the same shape. It adds a layer without hiding complexity.\\n\\nRemove it or move responsibility to the module that can complete the operation. Keep a forwarding boundary only when it adds policy, adaptation, or a distinct abstraction.\\n\\n## Split ownership\\n\\nMore than one module writes the same state or keeps its own copy of it. An agent that edits one writer can't see the others, so their rules diverge.\\n\\nGive each piece of state one owner. Other modules read it or ask the owner to change it.\\n\\n## Two ways to do one task\\n\\nThe design supports more than one way to do the same task. An agent copies whichever way it finds first, so every way keeps gaining callers.\\n\\nKeep one way. Move callers off the others and delete them in the same change.\\n\\n## Importable internals\\n\\nA caller can import a module's internals. An agent takes the shortest path that compiles, so it imports them directly and they become part of the interface.\\n\\nMake internals unreachable from outside the module, so an import from outside fails the build.\\n\\n## Hand-synced list\\n\\nTwo or more places list the same items, and adding an item means editing every list. An agent that sees one list updates only that one.\\n\\nKeep one list and derive the others from it. If a list can't be derived, make the build fail when the lists disagree.\\n0edd7dc5272e24f54d58bbacc63d59c516fde9c5\\n\"}}\n"
