Explains subsystem architecture, runtime flow, onboarding mental models. Use why for motivation.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# How\\n\\nExplore the codebase to answer \\\"how does X work?\\\" questions. Produce architectural explanations at the level of a senior engineer onboarding onto a subsystem, enough to build a working mental model, not so much that it reads like annotated source code.\\n\\nBefore dispatch, read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Bind worker operations to tools actually exposed and authorized in this session. Request the inherited parent model and effort unless a supported explicit selection is observed. Retain rejections and disclose replacements; an unavailable required selection is blocked. Record requested and observed selections separately, using unknown for unobserved values.\\n\\nKeep explorers and explainers read-only. Worker assignments record their role, distinct angle, exact input revision, identity and inspected results under the assignment contract. Reading a prompt does not register a worker. If independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, disclose reduced capability and perform the same exploration and explanation roles sequentially. Never claim parallel or independent exploration from sequential work. If source access is unavailable or denied, report the gap and limit the explanation to inspected evidence.\\n\\n## Step 1. Assess Complexity\\n\\nIf the scope is ambiguous, state your interpretation and explore. The user can redirect.\\n\\n- **Simple** (a single module, a small utility, a narrow question such as \\\"how does function X work\\\"): no explorers. One explainer explores and explains in a single pass. Go to Step 2b.\\n- **Complex** (a subsystem spanning multiple files or services, a cross-cutting feature, a full architectural overview): use parallel explorers when available, then hand off to the explainer. Go to Step 2a.\\n\\nWhen in doubt, take the simple path.\\n\\n## Step 2a. Explore (complex questions only)\\n\\nDecompose the question into 2 to 4 exploration angles, each a distinct slice of the subsystem. Dispatch all explorers together through an observed worker binding, with distinct assignments:\\n\\n- Role: how explorer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nEach explorer gets the prompt in `references/explorer-prompt.md` with its angle filled in. If workers are unavailable, explore those distinct angles sequentially and retain separate findings. Then go to Step 3.\\n\\n## Step 2b. Direct Explain (simple questions)\\n\\nAssign one explainer worker that explores and explains in one pass:\\n\\n- Role: how explainer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` without the explorer-findings section. The explainer reads and traces the source itself. If workers are unavailable, apply this role sequentially. Go to Step 4.\\n\\n## Step 3. Synthesize (complex questions only)\\n\\nOnce all explorer results have been inspected, assign one explainer worker to synthesize their findings into one explanation:\\n\\n- Role: how explainer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` with every explorer's findings filled in. In reduced capability, synthesize the sequential findings under the same read-only role.\\n\\n## Step 4. Present\\n\\nPresent the explainer's output to the user. Light edits for clarity or context from the conversation are fine. Do not substantially rewrite it.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nThe explanation uses the sections defined in `references/explainer-prompt.md`, dropping any that do not apply: Overview, Key Concepts, How It Works, Where Things Live, Gotchas.\\n---\\nname: architect\\ndescription: \\\"Sketch types, signatures, and module structure before code, then stay in the loop while implementation fills in. Use for /architect, 'architect this', 'design this', or non-trivial work where jumping to code would lock in the wrong shape.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Architect\\n\\nDesign before implementing. Sketch types, function signatures, class shapes, and module boundaries with `not implemented` bodies and pseudocode. Synthesize across multiple model perspectives, then fill in code against the chosen sketch. If implementation proves the sketch wrong, throw it out and redesign.\\n\\n## Start\\n\\nOpen a todolist with one entry per phase before starting. If no task-list tool is exposed, keep the same phase list in the response and disclose that limit.\\n\\n1. Ground\\n2. Sketch\\n3. Agree\\n4. Implement\\n5. Scrap\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Use only observed exposed tools within user authorization. Read and follow each required skill in full. If direct invocation is unavailable, disclose that limit and execute its complete workflow inline as reduced capability. A missing required skill remains blocked. Record worker identities, requested and observed selections, rejections, result artifacts and limits.\\n\\nIndependent exploration requires authorized workers with observed spawn and result inspection. Without workers, disclose reduced capability and produce at least two sequential, structurally distinct alternatives using the complete arena workflow and runner discipline. Sequential alternatives are not independent model perspectives. Unverified or identical observed models do not establish model diversity. Denied source reads block grounding; denied writes leave a read-only design and an explicit implementation block.\\n\\n## Phase A: Ground the problem\\n\\nBuild a real mental model of every system the new code touches. Run the **how** skill over the relevant subsystems.\\n\\nNaming a file isn't grounding. Produce the traced model `how` prescribes. If the design redefines ownership or layering, also run the **why** skill on the existing shape so the rationale becomes a constraint, not a guess.\\n\\nSkip Phase A only when the work is genuinely greenfield with no surrounding system to integrate.\\n\\n## Phase B: Sketch\\n\\nRun the **arena** skill with the design-sketch task and the Phase A grounding artifacts. Pass `references/runner-prompt.md` as each runner's prompt. Each candidate produces a design package shaped per `references/rationale-template.md`.\\n\\nTake runner preferences from an available user-supplied `architect runners` configuration, in place of arena runner preferences. When no architect runner preferences are supplied, request inherited selection per the model-role contract. Preserve the goal of distinct model perspectives when supported and verified; inheritance does not establish diversity. Resolve aliases and rejected entries through the complete **arena** Phase A rules and current model-role contract. Select explicit models only when the exposed operation supports them; otherwise disclose inherited selection and unverified diversity. If an exact selection is required but unavailable, block that selection instead of silently replacing it.\\n\\nDesign it twice. Require at least two structurally distinct candidates before synthesis, even when the first looks sufficient. This is the **exhaust-the-design-space** principle skill made concrete. Whole-shape alternatives, not point fixes inside one shape.\\n\\nScreen every candidate against [`references/design-red-flags.md`](references/design-red-flags.md) before synthesis. Assume the next contributor is an agent that sees only the files it opened, copies the nearest example, and takes the shortest path that compiles. Prefer the design where a change that looks right from one file is right for the whole repo.\\n\\nCompare viable candidates on interface depth. Prefer the design that hides more complexity behind a smaller, simpler public surface. A rich interface can keep call chains short by concentrating capability instead of scattering it across layers.\\n\\nArena returns one synthesized design package. The synthesis decision populates the rationale's \\\"Synthesis decision\\\" section.\\n\\n## Phase C: Agree (opt-in)\\n\\nDefault: proceed directly to implementation with the synthesized design when implementation is already authorized. No additional human checkpoint. Design-only scope does not authorize implementation.\\n\\nOpt in to a checkpoint when the invoker explicitly asks: \\\"/architect with checkpoint,\\\" \\\"stop and show me before implementing,\\\" or similar. Then surface the synthesized design and pause for sign-off.\\n\\nWith explicit commit authorization, the synthesis can ship as its own commit either way, as the \\\"scaffold first\\\" mode of the **foundational-thinking** principle skill. Planned and scoped breakage during fill-in is fine, per the **outcome-oriented-execution** principle skill. For adversarial pressure on the design before implementing, run the **interrogate** skill on the synthesized sketch.\\n\\nIf the human pushes back on the shape (in a checkpoint or after the fact), treat that as Phase A evidence. Re-ground and re-run Phase B before writing more code.\\n\\n## Phase D: Implement against the sketch\\n\\nReplace `not implemented` bodies with code, pseudocode with logic. The synthesized sketch is the contract.\\n\\nDeviations from the sketch are signal worth surfacing, not friction to absorb silently. If a function needs a parameter the sketch didn't anticipate, ask whether the sketch was wrong, the requirement was missed, or the implementation is overreaching.\\n\\n## Phase E: Scrap when the architecture is wrong\\n\\nIf implementation keeps producing friction the sketch can't absorb, throw the sketch out. Don't bolt fixes onto a wrong design, per the **redesign-from-first-principles** and **fix-root-causes** principle skills.\\n\\nThe signal is a *pattern*, not single instances. Tells:\\n\\n- The same shape of workaround appearing repeatedly across unrelated code.\\n- Multiple unrelated edge cases that all need special-case branches.\\n- Types that need escape hatches (`any`, casts, optional fields always set in practice) to compile.\\n- The \\\"we need a lock\\\" reflex when the sketch said the state wasn't shared.\\n- Callers having to know the abstraction's internal rules to use it.\\n- Two or more independent Phase D deviations of the same shape across the implementation.\\n\\nUse judgment. A few edge cases don't condemn an architecture. Some problems are legitimately complex. Complexity in the data is not complexity in the design.\\n\\nWhen you scrap:\\n\\n1. Re-run the **how** skill over what's been built.\\n2. Redesign as if the new constraints had been day-one assumptions, per redesign-from-first-principles.\\n3. Subtract before adding, per the **subtract-before-you-add** principle skill. The new sketch should be smaller than the old one before it grows.\\n4. Return to Phase B and re-run arena.\\n\\n## Outputs\\n\\nThe caller's usage is written first and the type sketch derived from it. One file with new types and signatures for small changes. Module map plus type definitions for larger work. The rationale ships alongside, shaped per `references/rationale-template.md`, including the usage sketch and the synthesis decision.\\n\\nApply [unslop](../unslop/SKILL.md) to generated rationale and reports without changing facts or principle meanings.\\n---\\nname: unslop\\ndescription: Cut AI tells from any writing. Must always apply.\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Unslop\\n\\nEdit text to remove AI patterns.\\n\\n## Process\\n\\n1. Scan for the patterns below.\\n2. Rewrite. Preserve meaning, match intended tone.\\n\\n## Patterns to detect and fix\\n\\nRule numbers are stable ids that other skills cite. A removed rule leaves a gap.\\n\\n### Content\\n\\n3. **Superficial -ing phrases.** \\\"highlighting...\\\", \\\"ensuring...\\\", \\\"reflecting...\\\", \\\"showcasing...\\\", \\\"fostering...\\\". Delete or expand with real sources.\\n5. **Vague attributions.** \\\"Experts believe\\\", \\\"Industry reports suggest\\\", \\\"Some critics argue\\\". Name the source or delete.\\n\\n### Language\\n\\n7. **AI vocabulary.** Additionally, crucial, delve, enduring, enhance, fostering, garner, interplay, intricate, landscape (abstract), pivotal, showcase, tapestry (abstract), testament, underscore, vibrant. Replace with plain words.\\n8. **Fancy ways to say \\\"is\\\".** \\\"serves as\\\", \\\"stands as\\\", \\\"boasts\\\", \\\"features\\\". Just say \\\"is\\\" or \\\"has\\\".\\n9. **\\\"Not just X, but Y.\\\"** State the point directly instead.\\n10. **Rule of three.** Forcing ideas into groups of three. Use the natural number.\\n11. **Synonym cycling.** Protagonist, main character, central figure, hero all in one paragraph. Pick one, repeat it.\\n12. **False ranges.** \\\"from X to Y\\\" where X and Y aren't on a meaningful scale. List topics directly.\\n\\n### Style\\n\\n13. **Em dash overuse.** Avoid em dashes entirely. Use periods or commas only (no parentheses, no en dashes, no hyphen-as-dash substitutes). If a thought needs separation, end the sentence or use a comma.\\n14. **Colon overuse.** Colons are fine before a list or example. Not as mid-sentence connectors. \\\"If you're coming from traditional automation: instead of registering event handlers, you describe conditions\\\" adds nothing with the colon. Rewrite to let the point stand on its own without comparison framing. \\\"Describing when the scheduler should fire works best as plain English.\\\" Same meaning, no crutch punctuation.\\n15. **Boldface overuse.** Don't bold every proper noun or acronym.\\n16. **Inline-header lists.** The tell is a bold label and colon that restates the line: \\\"**Performance:** Performance improved...\\\". Convert those to prose. A bold lead-in that ends in a period, names the item, and is followed by genuinely new detail (\\\"**Schema in TypeScript.** Tables live in one file.\\\") is fine, not a tell.\\n17. **Title case headings.** Use sentence case.\\n18. **Decorative emojis.** Remove from headings and bullets.\\n19. **Curly quotes.** Replace with straight quotes.\\n\\n### Communication artifacts\\n\\n20. **Chatbot phrases.** \\\"I hope this helps!\\\", \\\"Let me know if...\\\", \\\"Of course!\\\", \\\"Certainly!\\\", \\\"Found the smoking gun!\\\" Remove.\\n22. **Sycophantic tone.** \\\"Great question! You're absolutely right!\\\" Respond directly.\\n\\n### Filler\\n\\n23. **Filler phrases.** \\\"In order to\\\" becomes \\\"To\\\". \\\"Due to the fact that\\\" becomes \\\"Because\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" gets deleted.\\n24. **Excessive hedging.** \\\"could potentially possibly be argued that it might\\\" becomes \\\"may\\\".\\n25. **Generic conclusions.** \\\"The future looks bright.\\\" State specific plans or facts.\\n\\n### Jargon\\n\\n26. **Abstract metaphor nouns.** Substrate, wedge, vector, locus, vantage, nexus, primitive (as noun), harness (as metaphor), surface (as in \\\"API surface\\\"), bedrock, scaffolding (as metaphor), modality, paradigm, gold-plating, ratchet (as metaphor), evacuate (for moving code), endgame, north star, flywheel. These read as technical but usually have a plainer concrete word. \\\"Substrate\\\" becomes \\\"base\\\". \\\"Wedge in\\\" becomes \\\"add\\\". \\\"Vector\\\" becomes \\\"way\\\" or \\\"method\\\". \\\"Gold-plating\\\" becomes \\\"more than the job needs\\\". \\\"Ratchet\\\" becomes the mechanism's real name or \\\"a limit that only tightens\\\". \\\"Evacuate\\\" becomes \\\"move out\\\". \\\"Endgame\\\" becomes \\\"the last phase\\\". Pick the concrete word.\\n\\n### Plain speech\\n\\n27. **Say what it does, not how it feels.** \\\"the database stays close at hand\\\", \\\"SQL you can read\\\", \\\"types that follow your schema\\\" name a feeling. The fix names the mechanism or a number: \\\"`.toSQL()` returns the exact string sent to the database\\\", \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\". Ask what the sentence tells the reader to do or know, then write that. If you can't restate it as a concrete instruction, fact, or number, cut it. One more check: if the sentence could appear unchanged in another project's docs, it says nothing about this one. Cut it.\\n28. **Shorten or split dense sentences.** If the reader has to backtrack to parse a sentence, break it in two or drop clauses. One idea per sentence.\\n29. **Active voice.** Prefer it. Catch \\\"is/are/was/were + past participle\\\" and name the actor: \\\"queries are validated\\\" becomes \\\"the compiler validates queries\\\", \\\"the file is parsed by the loader\\\" becomes \\\"the loader parses the file\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or genuinely doesn't matter.\\n30. **Cut adverbs, or use a stronger verb.** \\\"runs quickly\\\" becomes \\\"is fast\\\" or the number. \\\"significantly improves\\\" becomes the measured delta. An adverb propping up a weak verb means the verb is wrong.\\n31. **Prefer the plain word.** \\\"utilize\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"leverage\\\" becomes \\\"use\\\", \\\"facilitate\\\" becomes \\\"help\\\", \\\"numerous\\\" becomes \\\"many\\\", \\\"in the event that\\\" becomes \\\"if\\\". The fancier synonym is rarely clearer.\\n32. **Mannered prose.** Metaphor or flourish where a literal phrase exists: aphorisms (\\\"wire it or delete it\\\"), rhetorical fragments for effect, personified code (\\\"the plan holds it\\\"), figurative verbs (\\\"rides along\\\", \\\"stands on\\\"), stock framing phrases. \\\"A dial worth turning\\\" becomes \\\"a parameter worth varying\\\". Say what you mean. Rule 26 covers the metaphor nouns.\\n33. **Over-compression.** Dropped articles, verbless fragments, symbol-speak, and abbreviations that make the reader decode instead of read. \\\"Parser rejects bad date → exit 2, no write\\\" becomes \\\"The parser rejects a bad date, exits with code 2, and writes nothing.\\\" Write whole sentences with their articles and verbs, and spell out arrows and abbreviations.\\n---\\nname: technical-writing\\ndescription: \\\"Layered technical-writing standard: Diátaxis structure, Google developer style sentences, STE instruction rules, Global English syntax. Use for /technical-writing or when writing or reviewing docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Technical writing\\n\\nThe goal is writing a tired engineer understands on the first read. Four layers get you there, one question each: what kind of document is this, how do sentences address the reader, how much does each sentence carry, and can any sentence be read two ways. Apply all four.\\n\\nThree rules sit above the layers:\\n\\n- **Cut every word that does no work.** If the sentence survives without a word, the word goes. \\\"In order to\\\" is \\\"to\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" is nothing.\\n- **Use the short, everyday word.** \\\"Use\\\", not \\\"utilize\\\". \\\"Help\\\", not \\\"facilitate\\\". \\\"Do\\\", not \\\"perform\\\". A long word has to buy its length with precision.\\n- **When a rule makes a sentence worse, fix the sentence another way or leave it alone.** The rules serve the reader. A sentence that follows every rule and sounds like a machine wrote it has failed.\\n\\nThe codebase is the word list. Write the real symbol, file, flag, or command name, not a synonym or a description of it.\\n\\nDon't invent jargon. Use the words a developer would say out loud: \\\"move\\\", \\\"delete\\\", \\\"a budget that only decreases\\\", not \\\"evacuate\\\", \\\"ratchet\\\", or \\\"endgame\\\". A named pattern is fine when the doc says what it means the first time. Propose a new offender and its replacement as an addition to `unslop`'s abstract-metaphor rule in your reply, with the diff. Don't edit that skill.\\n\\n## Vary the rhythm\\n\\nThe layers decide what a document says and how much each sentence carries. A doc can obey all of them and still read machine-written: every sentence clipped short, no view anywhere, nothing specific.\\n\\n- Mix sentence lengths on purpose. Short sentences land a point. Longer ones that take their time carry a fact with its condition or consequence.\\n- One thought per sentence does not mean one length per sentence. Split the sentence that carries two thoughts. Keep the long sentence that carries one.\\n- Have a view where the mode allows it. Explanation weighs trade-offs, so say what you make of them instead of listing pros and cons. Reference stays dry.\\n- Be specific over sterile. Not \\\"schema changes can cause issues\\\" but \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\".\\n\\n## Pick the mode first (Diátaxis)\\n\\nOne document, one mode. Two questions pick it: does the content inform action (doing) or understanding (thinking), and does it serve learning or work?\\n\\n- Action + learning: **tutorial**.\\n- Action + work: **how-to**.\\n- Understanding + work: **reference**.\\n- Understanding + learning: **explanation**.\\n\\nUse the compass on a whole document or on one sentence.\\n\\n**Tutorial: learning by doing.** You are the teacher. The learner's success is your job, not theirs. Open by saying what the learner will build, not what they will \\\"learn\\\". Every step produces a visible result, early and often. Tell them what they should see: the expected output, the prompt change, the log line. Cut explanation to one clause and a link. Teaching pauses break the lesson. Stay concrete. Write as \\\"we\\\", in commands: \\\"First, do x. Now, do y.\\\"\\n\\n**How-to: steps to a goal.** Solve a problem a person has, not an operation the machine can perform. Assume competence. Skip teaching. Action only: no digressions, no background, no completeness for its own sake. Link those instead. Allow forks and judgment: \\\"If you want x, do y.\\\" Name the guide by the task: \\\"How to calibrate the radar array\\\", not \\\"Radar array calibration\\\".\\n\\n**Reference: facts for lookup.** Describe. Only describe. No instruction, no persuasion, no opinion. Be dry, complete, and sure. State facts, options, limits, and errors with no hedging. Mirror the structure of the thing described, so code and docs can be navigated together. Put material where readers expect it. Generate from code where possible, so it stays true.\\n\\n**Explanation: understanding and why.** One bounded topic, readable away from the product. Each title should tolerate an implicit \\\"About...\\\" in front. Anchor on a real why question. Give context: design decisions, history, constraints, alternatives. Opinion is allowed here and nowhere else.\\n\\nDon't mix modes: no reference tables inside a tutorial, no tutorial hand-holding inside reference, no arguing inside a how-to. Split and link instead.\\n\\n## Write sentences to the reader (Google developer style)\\n\\n- Talk to the reader as \\\"you\\\", in the present tense. \\\"Will\\\" only for things that genuinely happen later.\\n- Say who does what: \\\"the compiler checks\\\", not \\\"is checked\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or beside the point.\\n- Write instructions as commands: \\\"Click Submit.\\\" State facts plainly. Never \\\"should be done\\\".\\n- Put the condition before the instruction: \\\"To delete the document, click Delete.\\\" The reader skips what does not apply.\\n- Put the common case first. Exceptions after.\\n- Sound like a knowledgeable friend. No buzzwords, no figurative language, no \\\"please\\\" in instructions, and never \\\"simply\\\", \\\"easy\\\", or \\\"quickly\\\" in a procedure. If it were simple, the reader would not be here.\\n- Don't pre-announce (\\\"we will soon support...\\\") and don't start consecutive sentences with the same phrase.\\n- Link with words that say where the link goes: the page title or a short description. Never \\\"click here\\\". Prefer a sentence of context on the page over a link off it.\\n- Headings carry the point, not just the topic (\\\"Pick the mode first\\\", not \\\"Modes\\\"). Sentence case. A task heading is a bare verb phrase (\\\"Create an instance\\\"). A concept heading is a noun phrase. One h1 per page, no skipped levels.\\n- Numbered lists for sequences, bullets for everything else. Introduce a list with a complete sentence. Keep items parallel.\\n- Code goes in code font. UI elements go in bold. Use serial commas. Drop \\\"etc.\\\" and say up front that a list is partial.\\n\\n## Make statements load one at a time (STE rules)\\n\\n- One instruction per sentence. One thought per sentence everywhere else.\\n- Split instructions longer than about 20 words and other sentences longer than about 25.\\n- Put the warning or condition before the step it guards: \\\"If hot oil touches your skin, injuries can occur.\\\"\\n- Keep \\\"the\\\" and \\\"a\\\": \\\"Remove backup file\\\" reads two ways. \\\"Remove the backup file\\\" reads one.\\n- Give each word one meaning and one job, then keep it. If \\\"check\\\" means inspect, don't also use it for restrain.\\n- Pick one word per action and stick to it: \\\"start\\\", not \\\"start\\\" here and \\\"initiate\\\" there.\\n- Write procedures as direct commands, never as narration and never in the passive: \\\"Install the component\\\", not \\\"the component must be installed\\\".\\n- Avoid \\\"-ing\\\" words where you can. They take too many grammatical jobs and breed misreadings.\\n\\n## Leave no sentence open to two readings (Global English)\\n\\n- Keep words like \\\"only\\\" and \\\"not\\\" next to the word they change: \\\"only fails on growth\\\" and \\\"fails only on growth\\\" say different things.\\n- Break up long noun strings: \\\"the proto import budget check script\\\" becomes \\\"the script that checks the proto-import budget\\\".\\n- Make every \\\"it\\\", \\\"they\\\", and \\\"this\\\" point at one obvious thing. Repeat the noun when in doubt. Never use \\\"this\\\" or \\\"which\\\" to point at a whole clause.\\n- Don't drop verbs: \\\"Phase 1 moves the converters and Phase 2 the runtime\\\" leaves Phase 2 without one. Give it one.\\n- Keep the small words that show structure. \\\"Ensure that the switch is off\\\" keeps \\\"that\\\" because it makes the sentence parse one way. Never trade clarity for word count.\\n- Repeat the article in a series when it prevents a misread: \\\"the client and the host\\\", not \\\"the client and host\\\", when they are two things.\\n- Say which parts \\\"and\\\" or \\\"or\\\" joins when a sentence can group two ways. \\\"Both...and\\\", \\\"either...or\\\", and \\\"if...then\\\" are free disambiguators.\\n- Use periods, not semicolons. Replace an em dash with a new sentence.\\n- Make text in parentheses a full grammatical unit or its own sentence. Never form plurals with \\\"(s)\\\".\\n- No slashes: write \\\"a, b, or both\\\" instead of \\\"a/b\\\" or \\\"and/or\\\".\\n- Call each thing by one name, everywhere. A doc that says \\\"the gate\\\", \\\"the ratchet\\\", and \\\"the budget check\\\" for one thing teaches three things. Rewording an unchanged sentence between edits costs the same way. Don't churn what didn't change.\\n- Skip idioms, colloquialisms, Latin abbreviations, and metaphors. A non-native reader, a translator, and an agent all parse plain constructions best.\\n\\n## Voice and repo specifics\\n\\n- Apply the **unslop** skill to every doc this skill touches. That skill owns the slop-pattern catalog: AI vocabulary, filler, hedging, formatting tells.\\n- PR descriptions and commit messages are writing too. Every layer except Diátaxis applies to them. A PR body is a briefing that a reviewer can read in under a minute. Do not paste swarm logs, SHA lists, or metric tables. Link them.\\n- Product UI strings are not documentation. Use your product's copy guidelines for those.\\n- Indent code snippets with tabs. Write real paths and real symbols. Make every count or tree claim true at the commit that lands it, and include the command that regenerates it.\\n\\n## Worked example\\n\\nBefore:\\n\\n> Configuration of the proto import ratchet budget script parameters is performed via budget.json. Note that it's important to remember that running with --write, which updates the committed budget to reflect the current count, should only be done when lowering it. If exceeded, CI fails.\\n\\nAfter:\\n\\n> `budget.mjs` reads the committed budget from `budget.json` and counts the files that import protos. If the count exceeds the budget, CI fails. Run `budget.mjs --write` only to lower the budget.\\n\\n## Access and permissions\\n\\nUse the current session capabilities in `../../references/host-capabilities.md` to read documents and verify authorized edits. Supplied text is enough for a rewrite in the conversation. If document writes are unavailable or denied, return the proposed text and state that it was not saved. If the document cannot be read and no text was supplied, report the missing input. Do not invent its content. Read `../unslop/SKILL.md` before applying it. If that dependency is unavailable, report the gap and label the review partial. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` as source metadata; host enforcement needs observed evidence. These limits also apply to PR descriptions and commit messages. Sending, publishing, or creating external changes requires explicit authorization.\\n---\\nname: principle-model-the-domain\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when writing stateful logic, or when code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure instead of scattered conditionals.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Model the Domain\\n\\nEncode the real domain in a data structure instead of scattering it across conditionals.\\n\\n**Why:** Scattered booleans, repeated shape assumptions, and branching spread across files are accidental complexity. A structure that matches the domain makes invalid states unrepresentable and deletes branches. Choosing it at write time is cheap. Recovering it later reads as a refactor and gets deferred.\\n\\n**Reach for structures like these:**\\n\\n- A state machine instead of scattered booleans, phases, or lifecycle checks.\\n- A typed object/model instead of loose parameters or repeated shape assumptions.\\n- A map, registry, lookup table, or discriminated union instead of branching spread across files.\\n- A reducer or command/event model instead of ad hoc state mutations.\\n- A module organized around one body of domain knowledge instead of a sequence such as load, validate, transform, and save. Execution order is not ownership.\\n- A small module boundary that gathers repeated behavior, ownership, or invariants.\\n- A queue, cache, index, graph/tree, or normalized collection where the data access pattern calls for it.\\n- Any other structure that fits. When none fits, work out what the code must never allow and how the data gets read, then find the structure that encodes exactly that.\\n\\nDo not force an abstraction. Prefer boring code if the current shape is already clear, local, and unlikely to grow. Be skeptical of an abstraction that adds indirection without removing branches, duplicated rules, invalid states, or lifecycle risk.\\n\\nThe sign that you skipped this is a new feature that grows an existing if/else chain by one more branch, or a second boolean that must stay in sync with the first. Temporal decomposition is another sign. Phase-named modules repeat the same domain rules across steps.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nFollow [session capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for source reads, writes and execution. Apply the principle within the authorized task. If writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced design proposal and state which implementation checks remain unperformed. If required domain rules or source are missing, identify them and keep the design provisional. Claim implementation or verification only for observed operations.\\n\\nPreserve explicit-invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: principle-foundational-thinking\\ndescription: \\\"Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Foundational Thinking\\n\\n**Structural decisions** protect option value. **Code-level decisions** protect simplicity.\\n\\n**Data structures first.** Get the data shape right before writing logic. Define core types early, trace every access pattern, and choose structures that match the dominant paths.\\n\\nAt code level, DRY the structure, not every line. Types and data models should converge. Three similar statements still beat a premature abstraction. Prefer explicit over clever. Test behavior and edge cases, not line counts.\\n\\n**Concurrency corollary.** Before sharing state between actors, ask \\\"what happens if another actor modifies this concurrently?\\\" If not \\\"nothing\\\", isolate.\\n\\n**Scaffold first.** If something helps every later phase, do it first. Ask \\\"does every subsequent phase benefit from this existing?\\\" CI, linting, test infrastructure, and shared types are scaffold. Sequence for option value: setup before features, tests before fixes. Keep commits small and single-purpose.\\n\\nEach increment should land a coherent abstraction or deepen one that exists. Do not spread a new capability across callers as special-case coordination.\\n\\nSubtraction comes before scaffolding. Remove dead code first, then lay foundations.\\n\\n## Capability and permission boundaries\\n\\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before inspecting source, editing files or running checks. Apply this principle within the authorized task. A recommendation to set up CI or make a commit grants no permission to perform it.\\n\\nIf source is available but writes or execution are unavailable, give a concrete design and work sequence as reduced capability. State which edits and checks were not performed. If required source or access patterns are missing, identify them and keep any proposed shape provisional. Claim implementation or verification only after observing the authorized operation.\\n---\\nname: principle-redesign-from-first-principles\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from day one, instead of bolting it on.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Redesign From First Principles\\n\\nWhen integrating a change, don't bolt it onto the existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been there from the start.\\n\\n- Read all affected files and understand the current design\\n- Ask: \\\"if we were writing this from scratch with this new requirement, what would we build?\\\"\\n- Propagate the change through every reference: types, docs, examples, rationale sections\\n- Think about the whole redesign, then deliver it incrementally\\n\\nThis is the method for preserving option value when integrating changes into an existing design.\\n\\n## Capability and permission boundaries\\n\\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before reading source, editing files or running checks. Apply the redesign within the authorized task. Propagating references does not grant permission to change unrelated files or perform external actions.\\n\\nIf source is available but writes or execution are unavailable, provide a concrete redesign and incremental sequence as reduced capability. State which edits and checks were not performed. If affected source or design context is missing, identify the missing inputs and keep any redesign provisional. Claim implementation or verification only after observing the authorized operation.\\n---\\nname: principle-subtract-before-you-add\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when sequencing an addition, refactor, or rewrite. Remove dead code, redundant validators, and stub references first, then build on the simpler base.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Subtract Before You Add\\n\\nWhen evolving a system, remove complexity first, then build.\\n\\n**Why:** Adding to a complex system compounds complexity. Removing first leaves less code, reveals the essential structure, and usually makes the next design obvious. Default to subtraction.\\n\\nMake simplification a continual investment. Leave the design slightly simpler and more capable behind the same or smaller surface than you found it.\\n\\n**The pattern:**\\n- Sequence removal before construction\\n- Cut before you polish (get to the minimum before investing in quality)\\n- Design for observed usage, not speculative edge cases\\n- No speculative validators, parsers, or guards beyond what the spec demands\\n- Simplify prompts (remove redundant instructions, excessive templates)\\n- When a reference has no novel content, delete it rather than leaving a stub\\n\\n## Session capabilities and limits\\n\\nUse [the capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to discover available source reads, writes and execution. Remove only behavior supported as obsolete by the inspected usage and requirements, within the authorized task.\\n\\nIf source is available but writes or execution are unavailable, provide an ordered removal and replacement proposal as reduced capability. State which changes and checks were not performed. If source or usage evidence is missing, identify the missing inputs before claiming removal is safe. Report only observed edits and checks.\\n\\nPreserve the invocation metadata above without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: principle-laziness-protocol\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Laziness Protocol\\n\\nAim for the most result with the least code and complexity.\\n\\n- **Prefer deletion.** When asked to refactor or improve, look for removals before additions.\\n- **Maintain a flat call hierarchy.** Avoid deep call chains. A rich interface that hides substantial work is not a deep call chain. If answering a question requires tracing through more than 3 files or layers, flatten it.\\n- **Consolidate decisions.** Do not repeat the same choice in several places. Put it behind one source of truth and pass the result as a simple flag.\\n- **Minimize the diff.** Make the smallest change that solves the problem. Fewer lines beat \\\"elegant\\\" boilerplate.\\n- **Question the threading.** If a task asks you to pass a new signal through types, schemas, pipelines, or similar layers, stop and look for a more direct path.\\n- **Sweat the small leaks.** Remove tiny pass-throughs, representation leaks, and duplicated choices before they spread. Small leaks compound into permanent coordination costs.\\n\\n**The test:** If a human developer would find the code exhausting to maintain, it is a bad solution.\\n\\n## Capability limits\\n\\nApply this principle within the authorized task. Follow [session capability checks](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before claiming file reads, edits or verification. When writes or execution are unavailable, label the read-only proposal as reduced capability and state the missing checks. If required source is unavailable, state that the refactor cannot be assessed. Structural validation does not prove runtime behavior.\\n---\\nname: principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when introducing a new internal API while old callers still exist. Migrate callers and delete the old API in the same wave instead of preserving compatibility layers.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Migrate Callers Then Delete Legacy APIs\\n\\nWhen we decide a new API is the right design, migrate callers and remove the old API in the same refactor wave instead of preserving compatibility layers.\\n\\n**Rule:**\\n- Do not keep legacy API paths only because internal callers still exist\\n- Inventory callers, migrate them, and delete the old API immediately\\n- Treat temporary adapters as exceptional and time-boxed, not default architecture\\n- Update tests to assert the new contract, and delete tests that only protect pre-refactor implementation details\\n\\n**When this applies:**\\n- No external users depend on backward compatibility\\n- The project can absorb coordinated breaking changes\\n- The new API is part of a simplification or refactor initiative\\n\\nKeeping both old and new APIs creates dual-path complexity, slows cleanup, and makes the codebase feel append-only.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before acting. Preserve user authorization and host permissions for caller edits, API deletion and test changes. The invocation metadata above is preserved source metadata; do not assume the host enforces it.\\n\\nInspect source and caller usage before deciding deletion is safe. Confirm the applicability conditions above, then review caller migration and legacy API removal together. If external compatibility or coordinated breaking changes are unresolved, identify the missing evidence or decision before deleting.\\n\\nIf writes or execution are unavailable, give a coordinated migration and deletion proposal as reduced capability. State which edits and checks were not performed. Missing source or caller evidence blocks safety and completion claims. Report completion only from observed changes and checks.\\n---\\nname: principle-prove-it-works\\ndescription: \\\"Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.'\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Prove It Works\\n\\nVerify every task output by checking the real thing directly. Do not infer from proxies, self-reports, or \\\"it compiles.\\\"\\n\\n**Why:** Unverified work has unknown correctness. Indirect verification (file mtimes, output freshness, agent self-reports, cached screenshots) feels cheaper than direct observation. Acting on a wrong inference costs far more than checking the source.\\n\\nCheck the real thing, not a proxy:\\n- Check process liveness directly, not indirectly through derived state\\n- Read the actual value, not a cached or derived representation\\n- When verification fails, suspect the observation method before suspecting the system\\n\\n## Script the check when you can\\n\\nThe strongest proof is a deterministic script that re-runs the same comparison, not a one-time eyeball. Write the script, run it, and keep its output as an artifact a reviewer can re-run instead of trusting your word.\\n\\nKeep the artifact visible for the human. Commit it only for large or complex work where the trail has to be auditable later, like a big port or migration (the **show-me-your-work** skill).\\n\\n## Session capabilities and permissions\\n\\nUse the exposed tools and observed operations described in [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md). Run checks and retain artifacts only within the user's authorized scope and host permissions. If direct inspection or execution is unavailable or denied, state which outputs remain unverified and withhold completion for those outputs. A proposed script is not evidence that it ran.\\n---\\nname: principle-minimize-reader-load\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when reviewing or shaping code that's hard to trace. Count layers between question and answer, and hidden state in the reader's head; collapse one-caller wrappers and shrink mutable scope.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Minimize Reader Load\\n\\nMaintainability is the work a reader must do to understand code. Track two axes:\\n1. **Layers to trace.** How many indirections sit between the question and the answer.\\n2. **State to hold.** How much hidden or mutable context the reader must keep in their head.\\n\\n**Why:** Code is read far more than it is written. LOC, cyclomatic complexity, and \\\"clean architecture\\\" are proxies. Reader load is the thing that matters. The two axes are independent. A flat file with 50 globals can be as hard to reason about as a 6-layer adapter stack. Guard both. This is the human analog of [Guard the Context Window](../principle-guard-the-context-window/SKILL.md). Working memory is finite for readers too.\\n\\n**The pattern:**\\n- **Collapse layers** that cost more than they save: wrappers with one caller, adapters with no second implementation, speculative indirection that was never needed. Inline them.\\n- **Make adjacent layers change the abstraction.** A layer that repeats the same methods and arguments adds reader load without compression. Collapse pass-through layers.\\n- **Demand interface compression.** A broad interface that hides little complexity makes readers learn both the surface and the implementation. Prefer boundaries that hide meaningful decisions.\\n- **Shrink state scope:** prefer pure functions (returns over mutations), locals over fields, fields over module state, and module state over globals. Derive instead of sync.\\n- **Name the invariant at the boundary,** not in every consumer, so the reader learns it once.\\n- Before adding a layer or a piece of state, ask: does this reduce reader load somewhere else by at least as much?\\n\\n**The test:** Can a new reader answer \\\"where does X come from?\\\" and \\\"what can change X?\\\" in under 30 seconds? If not, cut layers or cut state.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and permissions\\n\\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to discover available read, write and execution tools. Inspect the concrete path, its callers and mutable state before deciding which layers to collapse. Make edits only within the authorized task and available write permissions. Run appropriate checks only through observed execution tools.\\n\\nWhen source is readable but writes or execution are denied or unavailable, give a source-grounded proposal and identify the edits or checks you could not perform. Without source or sufficient caller and state evidence, report the missing evidence and stop concrete refactoring claims. Do not claim a measured reader time without observation. Preserve invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: principle-sequence-verifiable-units\\ndescription: \\\"Apply to multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and to how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a verifiable state, check each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself to a reviewer.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Sequence work into verifiable units\\n\\nOrder work as a sequence of small units, each ending in a state you can check, and don't advance until the current one is green.\\n\\n**Why:** A break caught at the unit that caused it is cheap to localize. A break caught after a batch is buried, and you have already built further on a broken base. Sequencing those same units into a delivery a reviewer can replay turns \\\"trust me\\\" into \\\"watch it go red, then green.\\\"\\n\\n**Execution.** In a sweep, migration, or any run of similar edits, verify each change before starting the next. Each unit is a before/after bracket: known-good state, one change, run the check, then proceed. Rebase onto clean trunk first so every check measures against the real baseline. When a lever does the edits, the per-unit check is nearly free. Run it anyway.\\n\\n**Delivery.** Stack commits and PRs in the order that proves the work. The canonical shape is the failing test first, then the fix on top. Other story orders are a subtraction before the reshape, a baseline capture before the treatment, the scaffold before the feature. Each commit lands on its own and the sequence reads as an argument.\\n\\nThe sequencing complement to the **prove-it-works** principle skill, which keeps each check real, and the **build-the-lever** principle skill, which makes the per-unit check cheap.\\n\\n## Capability and authorization boundary\\n\\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to establish available inspection and execution, and the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) for write ownership and authorization. Rebase onto clean trunk only when authorized and safe for the current work. Commits and PRs also require authorization and an available operation. If a required check cannot run, state that it remains unverified and block advancement; an unrun check is never green. A reduced workflow may provide the ordered units, specific checks and delivery plan while identifying unperformed operations. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` metadata without assuming host enforcement.\\n\"}}\n"

      "text": "Data shape is a finite Number and rows of objects with total. formatRows maps totals through formatInvoice. exportAmounts joins those strings with newline. Number.toFixed(2) owns display rounding. The lastFormatted variable is written then immediately returned, with no reader outside the function. fixed has one caller. This direct source explanation uses the how workflow sequentially because this worker may not spawn.\n"

    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/class-reverted.mjs",
      "bytes": 369,
      "sha256": "bf0206ae81c99c2761d7673c1508bf33d8845f158cd10198bf3908e851017089",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "export function formatAmount(value) {\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\n  return value.toFixed(2);\n}\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatAmount(row.total)); }\n// Separate known bug, outside formatter refactor: this sorts numeric strings lexically.\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\n"
    },

    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/checklist.md",
      "bytes": 4537,
      "sha256": "c9595304552acf1e0a80567082d6eb8a327779bb7588bdd64d2f5415230654bd",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "1. Pin the behavior contract first. Run the [how](../../how/SKILL.md) skill over the affected subsystem to learn the contract, then write a characterization test, snapshot, or equivalence harness that captures current behavior before any structure moves. If the area has no coverage, write the pin before touching structure. Type check and lint are not a pin.\n\n2. Name the structure the code is missing per [principle-model-the-domain](../../principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md). Boring code stays when the shape is already clear and local. The reshape must delete branches or invalid states, not add indirection.\n\n3. Name the target shape. State what the module layout, types, and call graph should be if built today ([principle-foundational-thinking](../../principle-foundational-thinking/SKILL.md), [principle-redesign-from-first-principles](../../principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md)). If the target crosses a function boundary, run the [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) skill for parallel design exploration of the shape before the move.\n\n4. Subtract before you add. Delete dead code, collapse one-caller wrappers, drop redundant validators, and remove orphan references before introducing the new shape ([principle-subtract-before-you-add](../../principle-subtract-before-you-add/SKILL.md)). The smallest change that reaches the target shape ships ([principle-laziness-protocol](../../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md)). A speculative cleanup that \"might help\" gets reverted.\n\n5. Move in small behavior-preserving steps, each keeping the pin green. For API reshapes, migrate every caller and delete the old API in the same wave ([principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis](../../principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis/SKILL.md)). No compatibility shims, no parallel old-and-new paths. Spot-check every rename against the actual files. Renames silently miss usages in strings, prose, and back-references. Delegate the mechanical edits to an authorized independent worker with a specific scope, including file paths, the names being moved and the behavior to hold. Supply the complete [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and record the exact input revision, owned output paths, worker identity and inspected artifacts. Use inherited model and effort unless a supported configured refactoring selection is observed. Record requested and actual selections separately; unknown actual models prove no diversity. Review the returned diff yourself. When workers are unavailable or unauthorized, perform authorized mechanical edits sequentially only as reduced capability and leave the independent delegation gate unsatisfied.\n\n6. Prove behavior is unchanged on the real artifact, not \"it compiles\" ([principle-prove-it-works](../../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md)). For larger reshapes, run an equivalence check: a script that diffs old-vs-new outputs, a recorded baseline replayed against the new code, or a smoke run on the matching surface via the relevant control skill.\n\n7. Confirm the change is worth keeping. The success measure is reduced reader load ([principle-minimize-reader-load](../../principle-minimize-reader-load/SKILL.md)). If the diff does not lower reader load somewhere, revert it.\n\n8. Rebase into small ordered commits. A subtraction commit, then the reshape, then any follow-on cleanup. Shape them with the [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md) principle skill, so each behavior-preserving slice stays green before the next. Rebase and commit only with authorization and an available operation, preserving concurrent work. Otherwise return the checked local diff and ordered delivery plan with these operations unperformed. Run **Opening a PR** by reading `skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/opening-a-pr.md` in full and following its complete contract when available and authorized. This is a deferred task 21 dependency for task 24 integration until that file exists; stop PR-dependent work if absent. No push or PR creation follows from this playbook without explicit authorization. Opening a PR never starts Babysit implicitly.\n\nReduced local checklist binding.\nBlocking first steps. Read sources and replay parent baseline.\nIndependent workstreams. None. One participant owns coupled formatter edits.\nShared mutable state. Only assigned work and worker-evidence paths.\nSmallest safe decomposition. One owner with a pin after each move.\nArchitect phases. Ground, Sketch, Agree, Implement, Scrap.\n"
    },

    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/after-subtraction.mjs",
      "bytes": 371,
      "sha256": "81c3ad5603429b52792f521259004166e2de6befac669c35545a4c59cb26f024",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "export function formatInvoice(value) {\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\n  return value.toFixed(2);\n}\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatInvoice(row.total)); }\n// Separate known bug, outside formatter refactor: this sorts numeric strings lexically.\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/design-review.md",
      "bytes": 508,
      "sha256": "0eae90d492aea7b8c11b7d04ef36d2825afd2cf41ff51e963a6450cb9c9647df",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "Candidate A keeps the finite-number boundary and literal toFixed call in a pure function. Candidate B adds a Formatter class, an instance, a field, and a forwarding function. The class hides no additional policy and adds lifecycle state. The pass-through and shallow-module screens reject B. Candidate A removes one call layer and one mutable module variable. These are source counts, not measured reading time. No parallel arena, independent judge, model diversity, or full architect completion is claimed.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/01-subtraction.json",

      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"step\": \"01-subtraction\",\n  \"command\": [\n    \"/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/runtime/bun-linux-x64/bun\",\n    \"/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/pin.mjs\"\n  ],\n  \"exitCode\": 0,\n  \"stdout\": \"[\\n  {\\n    \\\"input\\\": \\\"0\\\",\\n    \\\"value\\\": \\\"0.00\\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\\"input\\\": \\\"1.005\\\",\\n    \\\"value\\\": \\\"1.00\\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\\"input\\\": \\\"-1.005\\\",\\n    \\\"value\\\": \\\"-1.00\\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\\"input\\\": \\\"12.345\\\",\\n    \\\"value\\\": \\\"12.35\\\"\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\\"input\\\": \\\"NaN\\\",\\n    \\\"error\\\": {\\n      \\\"name\\\": \\\"Error\\\",\\n      \\\"message\\\": \\\"nonfinite\\\"\\n    }\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\\"input\\\": \\\"Infinity\\\",\\n    \\\"error\\\": {\\n      \\\"name\\\": \\\"Error\\\",\\n      \\\"message\\\": \\\"nonfinite\\\"\\n    }\\n  },\\n  {\\n    \\\"emptyRows\\\": [],\\n    \\\"rows\\\": [\\n      \\\"1.00\\\",\\n      \\\"12.35\\\"\\n    ],\\n    \\\"csv\\\": \\\"0.00\\\\n-1.00\\\",\\n    \\\"knownUnrelatedSort\\\": [\\n      \\\"10\\\",\\n      \\\"2\\\"\\n    ]\\n  }\\n]\\n\",\n  \"stderr\": \"\",\n  \"formatterSha256\": \"81c3ad5603429b52792f521259004166e2de6befac669c35545a4c59cb26f024\"\n}"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/before-formatter.mjs",
      "bytes": 475,
      "sha256": "405007871ab7b0d2fbe1363eff4d19a26d7f316e77cf8fbf818cf051101d9fa7",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "let lastFormatted = '';\nfunction fixed(value) { return value.toFixed(2); }\nexport function formatInvoice(value) {\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\n  lastFormatted = fixed(value);\n  return lastFormatted;\n}\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatInvoice(row.total)); }\n// Separate known bug, outside formatter refactor: this sorts numeric strings lexically.\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/class-added.mjs",
      "bytes": 460,
      "sha256": "0b208a5385e72742383b34452f9aa65a18b97c009cf196650c3c980803af9344",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "class Formatter {\n  lastFormatted = '';\n  format(value) {\n    if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\n    this.lastFormatted = value.toFixed(2);\n    return this.lastFormatted;\n  }\n}\nconst formatter = new Formatter();\nexport function formatAmount(value) { return formatter.format(value); }\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatAmount(row.total)); }\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\n"
    },

      "text": "{\"request\":{\"cmd\":\"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/refactoring.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/how/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/how/references/explorer-prompt.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/how/references/explainer-prompt.md; cat /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/task-input/inputs/additional/invoice/*\",\"max_output_tokens\":14000},\"response\":{\"chunk_id\":\"9b28b5\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000032981,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":4486,\"output\":\"### Refactoring\\n\\nRead the complete [mode](../SKILL.md) and each applied principle leaf before acting. Copy all eight steps verbatim into the available todo list, or an authorized local checklist with a disclosed reduced binding, before task-specific items. Discover [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), follow [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Missing required reads or operations stop dependent work. Preserve the mode's companion, comments and matching-control gates.\\n\\n**You own the contract. The structure changes. The behavior does not.** Distinct from Feature, which adds behavior, and Bug fix, which corrects it.\\n\\nIf the cleanup reveals a missing feature or a real bug, split it out and ship the structural change first against the pinned contract. A redesign is allowed, but name it and route to Feature. Large or cross-cutting structural work belongs to the [figure-it-out](../../figure-it-out/SKILL.md) skill. This playbook is the focused-to-medium change.\\n\\n1. Pin the behavior contract first. Run the [how](../../how/SKILL.md) skill over the affected subsystem to learn the contract, then write a characterization test, snapshot, or equivalence harness that captures current behavior before any structure moves. If the area has no coverage, write the pin before touching structure. Type check and lint are not a pin.\\n2. Name the structure the code is missing per [principle-model-the-domain](../../principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md). Boring code stays when the shape is already clear and local. The reshape must delete branches or invalid states, not add indirection.\\n3. Name the target shape. State what the module layout, types, and call graph should be if built today ([principle-foundational-thinking](../../principle-foundational-thinking/SKILL.md), [principle-redesign-from-first-principles](../../principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md)). If the target crosses a function boundary, run the [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) skill for parallel design exploration of the shape before the move.\\n4. Subtract before you add. Delete dead code, collapse one-caller wrappers, drop redundant validators, and remove orphan references before introducing the new shape ([principle-subtract-before-you-add](../../principle-subtract-before-you-add/SKILL.md)). The smallest change that reaches the target shape ships ([principle-laziness-protocol](../../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md)). A speculative cleanup that \\\"might help\\\" gets reverted.\\n5. Move in small behavior-preserving steps, each keeping the pin green. For API reshapes, migrate every caller and delete the old API in the same wave ([principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis](../../principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis/SKILL.md)). No compatibility shims, no parallel old-and-new paths. Spot-check every rename against the actual files. Renames silently miss usages in strings, prose, and back-references. Delegate the mechanical edits to an authorized independent worker with a specific scope, including file paths, the names being moved and the behavior to hold. Supply the complete [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and record the exact input revision, owned output paths, worker identity and inspected artifacts. Use inherited model and effort unless a supported configured refactoring selection is observed. Record requested and actual selections separately; unknown actual models prove no diversity. Review the returned diff yourself. When workers are unavailable or unauthorized, perform authorized mechanical edits sequentially only as reduced capability and leave the independent delegation gate unsatisfied.\\n6. Prove behavior is unchanged on the real artifact, not \\\"it compiles\\\" ([principle-prove-it-works](../../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md)). For larger reshapes, run an equivalence check: a script that diffs old-vs-new outputs, a recorded baseline replayed against the new code, or a smoke run on the matching surface via the relevant control skill.\\n7. Confirm the change is worth keeping. The success measure is reduced reader load ([principle-minimize-reader-load](../../principle-minimize-reader-load/SKILL.md)). If the diff does not lower reader load somewhere, revert it.\\n8. Rebase into small ordered commits. A subtraction commit, then the reshape, then any follow-on cleanup. Shape them with the [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md) principle skill, so each behavior-preserving slice stays green before the next. Rebase and commit only with authorization and an available operation, preserving concurrent work. Otherwise return the checked local diff and ordered delivery plan with these operations unperformed. Run **Opening a PR** by reading `skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/opening-a-pr.md` in full and following its complete contract when available and authorized. This is a deferred task 21 dependency for task 24 integration until that file exists; stop PR-dependent work if absent. No push or PR creation follows from this playbook without explicit authorization. Opening a PR never starts Babysit implicitly.\\n\\n**Reply:** the structure that changed, the pin you held it against, the equivalence proof, the reader-load delta, what shipped and what got reverted. No new behavior.\\n\\nIf writes or execution are unavailable, return a source-grounded proposal and identify the unperformed pin, edits or equivalence checks. Missing behavior evidence blocks unchanged-behavior claims. Structural validation, type checks, lint and a worker self-report never prove equivalence. Report each missing capability and denied gate alongside the partial artifacts.\\n---\\nname: how\\ndescription: \\\"Use for \\\\\\\"how does X work\\\\\\\", code walkthroughs before changing something, and placement / ownership / layering questions (\\\\\\\"where should this live\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"which package owns this\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"is this the right layer\\\\\\\"). Explains subsystem architecture, runtime flow, onboarding mental models. Use why for motivation.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# How\\n\\nExplore the codebase to answer \\\"how does X work?\\\" questions. Produce architectural explanations at the level of a senior engineer onboarding onto a subsystem, enough to build a working mental model, not so much that it reads like annotated source code.\\n\\nBefore dispatch, read [session capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Bind worker operations to tools actually exposed and authorized in this session. Request the inherited parent model and effort unless a supported explicit selection is observed. Retain rejections and disclose replacements; an unavailable required selection is blocked. Record requested and observed selections separately, using unknown for unobserved values.\\n\\nKeep explorers and explainers read-only. Worker assignments record their role, distinct angle, exact input revision, identity and inspected results under the assignment contract. Reading a prompt does not register a worker. If independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, disclose reduced capability and perform the same exploration and explanation roles sequentially. Never claim parallel or independent exploration from sequential work. If source access is unavailable or denied, report the gap and limit the explanation to inspected evidence.\\n\\n## Step 1. Assess Complexity\\n\\nIf the scope is ambiguous, state your interpretation and explore. The user can redirect.\\n\\n- **Simple** (a single module, a small utility, a narrow question such as \\\"how does function X work\\\"): no explorers. One explainer explores and explains in a single pass. Go to Step 2b.\\n- **Complex** (a subsystem spanning multiple files or services, a cross-cutting feature, a full architectural overview): use parallel explorers when available, then hand off to the explainer. Go to Step 2a.\\n\\nWhen in doubt, take the simple path.\\n\\n## Step 2a. Explore (complex questions only)\\n\\nDecompose the question into 2 to 4 exploration angles, each a distinct slice of the subsystem. Dispatch all explorers together through an observed worker binding, with distinct assignments:\\n\\n- Role: how explorer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nEach explorer gets the prompt in `references/explorer-prompt.md` with its angle filled in. If workers are unavailable, explore those distinct angles sequentially and retain separate findings. Then go to Step 3.\\n\\n## Step 2b. Direct Explain (simple questions)\\n\\nAssign one explainer worker that explores and explains in one pass:\\n\\n- Role: how explainer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` without the explorer-findings section. The explainer reads and traces the source itself. If workers are unavailable, apply this role sequentially. Go to Step 4.\\n\\n## Step 3. Synthesize (complex questions only)\\n\\nOnce all explorer results have been inspected, assign one explainer worker to synthesize their findings into one explanation:\\n\\n- Role: how explainer\\n- Model and effort: follow the model-role contract\\n- Access: read-only\\n\\nBuild its prompt from `references/explainer-prompt.md` with every explorer's findings filled in. In reduced capability, synthesize the sequential findings under the same read-only role.\\n\\n## Step 4. Present\\n\\nPresent the explainer's output to the user. Light edits for clarity or context from the conversation are fine. Do not substantially rewrite it.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nThe explanation uses the sections defined in `references/explainer-prompt.md`, dropping any that do not apply: Overview, Key Concepts, How It Works, Where Things Live, Gotchas.\\n# Explorer Prompt Template\\n\\nBuild each explorer worker's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are exploring a codebase to understand how something works. Gather facts. Trace code paths, read implementations, map components. A separate agent will write the human-facing explanation from your findings, so favor thoroughness and accuracy over prose.\\n\\nWhen independent workers are available, other explorers investigate different slices in parallel. In reduced capability, the coordinator explores distinct slices sequentially and discloses that limit. Don't try to cover everything. Focus on your assigned angle and go deep.\\n\\n## Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## Your Exploration Angle\\n\\n{EXPLORATION_ANGLE}\\n\\n## Exploration Instructions\\n\\nStart by finding the relevant code. Use available file discovery, symbol search and file-reading tools to understand the actual implementation. Don't guess from names. Read the code.\\n\\nFollow this pattern:\\n1. **Find the entry point.** What triggers this behavior? A user action, an API call, a scheduled job? Find where it starts.\\n2. **Trace the flow.** Follow the call chain from the entry point. Read each function. Understand what data flows through and how it transforms.\\n3. **Map the key abstractions.** What types, interfaces, services, or classes are central? Read their definitions. Understand what they represent and why they exist.\\n4. **Find the boundaries.** Where does this subsystem interface with others? What goes in, what comes out?\\n5. **Look for the non-obvious.** Anything surprising? Anything that looks like a historical artifact? Anything a newcomer would misunderstand?\\n\\nKeep exploring until you can describe the full picture without hand-waving. If you hit a part you can't trace, say so explicitly. \\\"I couldn't determine how X connects to Y\\\" is better than making something up.\\n\\n## Output\\n\\nReturn your findings in this structure. Be factual and specific. Reference exact file paths, function names, type names, and line numbers where relevant.\\n\\n### Components Found\\nThe key types, services, classes, and abstractions. For each: name, file path, and a one-sentence description of what it does.\\n\\n### Flow\\nThe execution flow step by step. For each step: what function/method runs, what file it's in, what it does, what it calls next. Include the data that flows between steps.\\n\\n### Files Read\\nEvery file you read during exploration, so the explainer can reference them.\\n\\n### Boundaries\\nWhere this subsystem connects to other parts of the codebase. The inputs and outputs.\\n\\n### Non-Obvious Things\\nAnything surprising, historically motivated, or easy to get wrong. Things that look like they should work one way but work another.\\n\\n### Open Questions\\nAnything you couldn't fully trace or understand. Be honest about gaps.\\n# Explainer Prompt Template\\n\\nBuild the explainer worker's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are writing an architectural explanation for a senior engineer. For complex questions, explorers have traced different slices and gathered findings, independently when supported or sequentially with an explicit reduced-capability disclosure. For simple questions, omit Explorer Findings and explore the source yourself in this role. Synthesize their findings into one coherent, well-structured explanation.\\n\\n## Original Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## Explorer Findings\\n\\n{EXPLORER_FINDINGS_ALL}\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\nThe explorers each investigated a different angle of the same subsystem. Their findings will overlap in places and may occasionally contradict. Reconcile them. Merge overlapping descriptions, resolve contradictions by checking the code yourself, and combine the separate slices into a unified picture.\\n\\nWrite an explanation a senior engineer unfamiliar with this area could read and walk away with a solid mental model, understanding the architecture well enough to start working in it confidently.\\n\\nYou have read-only access to the codebase to check anything, clarify a detail, or fill a gap. Use available file reading, symbol search and file discovery as needed. When explorer findings are supplied, you should not need to re-explore from scratch. Without them, trace entry points, calls, data, abstractions and boundaries yourself; disclose anything you cannot trace.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nUse this structure, adapted to what makes sense for the question. Not every section is needed for every question.\\n\\n### Overview\\n1-2 paragraphs. What is this thing, what does it do, why does it exist. Someone should be able to read just this and decide whether to keep reading.\\n\\n### Key Concepts\\nThe important types, services, or abstractions needed to follow the rest. Brief definitions, not exhaustive.\\n\\n### How It Works\\nThe core of the explanation, and the longest section. Walk through the flow: what triggers it, what happens step by step, where data goes, what the decision points are.\\n\\nUse prose, not pseudocode. Reference specific files and functions so the reader knows where to look, but don't dump large code blocks unless a snippet is essential to a point.\\n\\nWhen the flow involves multiple components talking to each other, or data transforming through stages, include a diagram. Use mermaid (```mermaid) for structured flows (sequence diagrams, flowcharts, component graphs) or ASCII art for simpler relationships where mermaid would be overkill. Use your judgment. A diagram should clarify, not decorate. If prose covers the flow, skip the diagram.\\n\\n### Where Things Live\\nA brief file/directory map. Just the ones someone would need to start working here.\\n\\n### Gotchas\\nNon-obvious things, surprising behavior, historical context, pitfalls. Skip this section if there's nothing worth calling out.\\n\\n## Communication Style\\n\\n- Use concrete language, not abstractions-about-abstractions\\n- Say \\\"the `UserService` calls `AuthClient.refresh()`\\\" not \\\"the service delegates to the client\\\"\\n- When something is complex, explain why it's complex. Don't just describe the complexity\\n- When something is simple, don't pad it out\\n- If there's a helpful analogy, use it. If there isn't, don't force one\\n- If the explorers flagged open questions or gaps, acknowledge them rather than hiding them\\n# Synthetic invoice decision record\\n\\nDisplay contract: JavaScript Number.toFixed(2), including its binary floating-point rounding behavior. Do not silently replace this with accounting decimal rules. Empty row lists produce []. Nonfinite amounts throw Error('nonfinite'). Capture observed outputs before edits for 0, 1.005, -1.005, 12.345, empty rows, NaN and Infinity. Output examples in a test are expectations, not proof a test ran.\\n\\nFixture history decision: a UI consumer compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports formatRows. This is why rounding belongs at the display boundary. No customer statement or external ticket was retrieved. Errors are thrown by formatInvoice and are not swallowed by the controller. Alternative A removes fixed and lastFormatted; alternative B introduces a stateful Formatter class. Compare reader cost and behavioral equivalence.\\n\\nAuthorized internal rename target for preserved-behavior: formatInvoice -> formatAmount. Migrate imports, docs and string references together. sortAmounts is a known unrelated defect and must remain separate. Calculator cache is observable through inspectCachedTotal; changing it requires checking that consumer.\\nimport { formatRows } from './formatter.mjs';\\nexport function exportAmounts(rows) { return formatRows(rows).join('\\\\n'); }\\nexport const publicFixtureSymbols = ['formatInvoice', 'formatRows'];\\nlet lastFormatted = '';\\nfunction fixed(value) { return value.toFixed(2); }\\nexport function formatInvoice(value) {\\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\\n  lastFormatted = fixed(value);\\n  return lastFormatted;\\n}\\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatInvoice(row.total)); }\\n// Separate known bug, outside formatter refactor: this sorts numeric strings lexically.\\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\\n\"}}\n"
