    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/checklist.md",
      "bytes": 5789,
      "sha256": "58bc65507e8370137abb7f039fea6c02d8cb2cfde0e99abc0ca338193f52c3cc",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "### Refactoring\n\nRead the complete [mode](../SKILL.md) and each applied principle leaf before acting. Copy all eight steps verbatim into the available todo list, or an authorized local checklist with a disclosed reduced binding, before task-specific items. Discover [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md), follow [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Missing required reads or operations stop dependent work. Preserve the mode's companion, comments and matching-control gates.\n\n**You own the contract. The structure changes. The behavior does not.** Distinct from Feature, which adds behavior, and Bug fix, which corrects it.\n\nIf the cleanup reveals a missing feature or a real bug, split it out and ship the structural change first against the pinned contract. A redesign is allowed, but name it and route to Feature. Large or cross-cutting structural work belongs to the [figure-it-out](../../figure-it-out/SKILL.md) skill. This playbook is the focused-to-medium change.\n\n1. Pin the behavior contract first. Run the [how](../../how/SKILL.md) skill over the affected subsystem to learn the contract, then write a characterization test, snapshot, or equivalence harness that captures current behavior before any structure moves. If the area has no coverage, write the pin before touching structure. Type check and lint are not a pin.\n2. Name the structure the code is missing per [principle-model-the-domain](../../principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md). Boring code stays when the shape is already clear and local. The reshape must delete branches or invalid states, not add indirection.\n3. Name the target shape. State what the module layout, types, and call graph should be if built today ([principle-foundational-thinking](../../principle-foundational-thinking/SKILL.md), [principle-redesign-from-first-principles](../../principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md)). If the target crosses a function boundary, run the [architect](../../architect/SKILL.md) skill for parallel design exploration of the shape before the move.\n4. Subtract before you add. Delete dead code, collapse one-caller wrappers, drop redundant validators, and remove orphan references before introducing the new shape ([principle-subtract-before-you-add](../../principle-subtract-before-you-add/SKILL.md)). The smallest change that reaches the target shape ships ([principle-laziness-protocol](../../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md)). A speculative cleanup that \"might help\" gets reverted.\n5. Move in small behavior-preserving steps, each keeping the pin green. For API reshapes, migrate every caller and delete the old API in the same wave ([principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis](../../principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis/SKILL.md)). No compatibility shims, no parallel old-and-new paths. Spot-check every rename against the actual files. Renames silently miss usages in strings, prose, and back-references. Delegate the mechanical edits to an authorized independent worker with a specific scope, including file paths, the names being moved and the behavior to hold. Supply the complete [poteto role prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) and record the exact input revision, owned output paths, worker identity and inspected artifacts. Use inherited model and effort unless a supported configured refactoring selection is observed. Record requested and actual selections separately; unknown actual models prove no diversity. Review the returned diff yourself. When workers are unavailable or unauthorized, perform authorized mechanical edits sequentially only as reduced capability and leave the independent delegation gate unsatisfied.\n6. Prove behavior is unchanged on the real artifact, not \"it compiles\" ([principle-prove-it-works](../../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md)). For larger reshapes, run an equivalence check: a script that diffs old-vs-new outputs, a recorded baseline replayed against the new code, or a smoke run on the matching surface via the relevant control skill.\n7. Confirm the change is worth keeping. The success measure is reduced reader load ([principle-minimize-reader-load](../../principle-minimize-reader-load/SKILL.md)). If the diff does not lower reader load somewhere, revert it.\n8. Rebase into small ordered commits. A subtraction commit, then the reshape, then any follow-on cleanup. Shape them with the [sequence-verifiable-units](../../principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md) principle skill, so each behavior-preserving slice stays green before the next. Rebase and commit only with authorization and an available operation, preserving concurrent work. Otherwise return the checked local diff and ordered delivery plan with these operations unperformed. Run **Opening a PR** by reading `skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/opening-a-pr.md` in full and following its complete contract when available and authorized. This is a deferred task 21 dependency for task 24 integration until that file exists; stop PR-dependent work if absent. No push or PR creation follows from this playbook without explicit authorization. Opening a PR never starts Babysit implicitly.\n\n**Reply:** the structure that changed, the pin you held it against, the equivalence proof, the reader-load delta, what shipped and what got reverted. No new behavior.\n\nIf writes or execution are unavailable, return a source-grounded proposal and identify the unperformed pin, edits or equivalence checks. Missing behavior evidence blocks unchanged-behavior claims. Structural validation, type checks, lint and a worker self-report never prove equivalence. Report each missing capability and denied gate alongside the partial artifacts.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/work/exporter.mjs",
      "bytes": 190,
      "sha256": "22018eca9aab2ac09a5e9319751d5c6d9a171b30812748a274d5c331d8c6b392",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { formatRows } from './formatter.mjs';\nexport function exportAmounts(rows) { return formatRows(rows).join('\\n'); }\nexport const publicFixtureSymbols = ['formatAmount', 'formatRows'];\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/work/decision.md",
      "bytes": 1171,
      "sha256": "349a4eefd2225130fd63cf4e1c23d66b95adf8095530761724a955099b6b8c04",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Synthetic invoice decision record\n\nDisplay contract: JavaScript Number.toFixed(2), including its binary floating-point rounding behavior. Do not silently replace this with accounting decimal rules. Empty row lists produce []. Nonfinite amounts throw Error('nonfinite'). Capture observed outputs before edits for 0, 1.005, -1.005, 12.345, empty rows, NaN and Infinity. Output examples in a test are expectations, not proof a test ran.\n\nFixture history decision: a UI consumer compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports formatRows. This is why rounding belongs at the display boundary. No customer statement or external ticket was retrieved. Errors are thrown by formatAmount and are not swallowed by the controller. Alternative A removes fixed and lastFormatted; alternative B introduces a stateful Formatter class. Compare reader cost and behavioral equivalence.\n\nAuthorized internal API name for preserved-behavior: formatAmount. Imports, docs and string references use the same name. sortAmounts is a known unrelated defect and must remain separate. Calculator cache is observable through inspectCachedTotal; changing it requires checking that consumer.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/work/formatter.mjs",
      "bytes": 369,
      "sha256": "bf0206ae81c99c2761d7673c1508bf33d8845f158cd10198bf3908e851017089",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "export function formatAmount(value) {\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\n  return value.toFixed(2);\n}\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatAmount(row.total)); }\n// Separate known bug, outside formatter refactor: this sorts numeric strings lexically.\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/lead-candidate-read.raw.json",
      "bytes": 3348,
      "sha256": "f32da247974f1e6b7af3338c87139fdc66875e225b761ba485f3ce73705e2c06",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\"request\":{\"cmd\":\"cat /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/candidate-A.mjs /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/candidate-B.mjs /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/design-review.md /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/grounding.md /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/class-added.mjs /mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior/worker-evidence/class-reverted.mjs\",\"max_output_tokens\":5000},\"response\":{\"chunk_id\":\"0e6ea0\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000035012,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":648,\"output\":\"export function formatInvoice(value) {\\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\\n  return value.toFixed(2);\\n}\\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatInvoice(row.total)); }\\n// Separate known bug, outside formatter refactor: this sorts numeric strings lexically.\\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\\nclass Formatter {\\n  lastFormatted = '';\\n  format(value) {\\n    if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\\n    this.lastFormatted = value.toFixed(2);\\n    return this.lastFormatted;\\n  }\\n}\\nconst formatter = new Formatter();\\nexport function formatInvoice(value) { return formatter.format(value); }\\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatInvoice(row.total)); }\\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\\nCandidate A keeps the finite-number boundary and literal toFixed call in a pure function. Candidate B adds a Formatter class, an instance, a field, and a forwarding function. The class hides no additional policy and adds lifecycle state. The pass-through and shallow-module screens reject B. Candidate A removes one call layer and one mutable module variable. These are source counts, not measured reading time. No parallel arena, independent judge, model diversity, or full architect completion is claimed.\\nData shape is a finite Number and rows of objects with total. formatRows maps totals through formatInvoice. exportAmounts joins those strings with newline. Number.toFixed(2) owns display rounding. The lastFormatted variable is written then immediately returned, with no reader outside the function. fixed has one caller. This direct source explanation uses the how workflow sequentially because this worker may not spawn.\\nclass Formatter {\\n  lastFormatted = '';\\n  format(value) {\\n    if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\\n    this.lastFormatted = value.toFixed(2);\\n    return this.lastFormatted;\\n  }\\n}\\nconst formatter = new Formatter();\\nexport function formatAmount(value) { return formatter.format(value); }\\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatAmount(row.total)); }\\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\\nexport function formatAmount(value) {\\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\\n  return value.toFixed(2);\\n}\\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatAmount(row.total)); }\\n// Separate known bug, outside formatter refactor: this sorts numeric strings lexically.\\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\\n\"}}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/pin.mjs",
      "bytes": 1115,
      "sha256": "2ba86148e3552464c3d8341f4a293fdd066b8e78f161379cc2af5f578b2355a4",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { writeFileSync, readFileSync } from 'node:fs';\nconst root='/mnt/data/pstack-013-evaluation/outputs/preserved-behavior';\nconst module=await import(`${root}/work/formatter.mjs`);\nconst {exportAmounts,publicFixtureSymbols}=await import(`${root}/work/exporter.mjs`);\nconst format=module.formatAmount??module.formatInvoice;\nconst observations=[];\nfor(const value of [0,1.005,-1.005,12.345,NaN,Infinity]) {try{observations.push({input:String(value),value:format(value)});}catch(error){observations.push({input:String(value),error:{name:error.name,message:error.message}});}}\nobservations.push({emptyRows:module.formatRows([]),rows:module.formatRows([{total:1.005},{total:12.345}]),csv:exportAmounts([{total:0},{total:-1.005}]),knownUnrelatedSort:module.sortAmounts(['10','2'])});\nconsole.log(JSON.stringify(observations,null,2));\nif(process.argv[2]==='baseline')writeFileSync(`${root}/baseline-observations.json`,JSON.stringify(observations,null,2));\nelse if(JSON.stringify(observations)!==JSON.stringify(JSON.parse(readFileSync(`${root}/baseline-observations.json`,'utf8'))))throw new Error('Behavior changed');\n"
    },

Rename its internal API and migrate code, string references, docs and back-references together. One fixture exposes an unrelated bug, which must stay separate. A speculative abstraction adds reader load and must be reverted. Permit local edits and execution but no external writes. Repeat with workers unavailable, execution denied and behavior coverage absent. Return the changed structure, held pin, equivalence proof, reader-load delta, shipped and reverted work.\",\n    \"requirements\": [\n      \"Read the full mode and eight-step Refactoring playbook, required leaves and routed companion contracts. Record steps before task-specific todos. Route large or cross-cutting work to figure-it-out and named redesigns that change behavior to Feature. Split newly discovered bugs or features out and deliver the structural change first against its pinned contract.\",\n      \"Use how to trace current behavior, then create and execute characterization tests, snapshots or an equivalence harness before moving structure. Write coverage first if absent. Type checks and lint are insufficient pins.\",\n      \"Name the missing domain structure and target module layout, types and call graph. Keep clear local boring code; new shape deletes branches or invalid states. Crossing a function boundary requires complete architect exploration with its runner prompts, rationale and candidate screening.\",\n      \"Subtract dead code, one-caller wrappers, redundant validators and orphan references before additions. Keep the smallest change that reaches the target and revert speculation.\",\n      \"Each small move keeps the original pin green. Migrate every API caller and delete the old API in the same wave without compatibility shims or parallel paths. Inspect actual files for rename usages in strings, prose and back-references. Delegate mechanical edits with the complete poteto prompt, exact revision, ownership and observed inherited or supported configured selection. Inspect artifacts and review the diff.\",\n      \"Verify unchanged behavior on the real artifact. Larger reshapes require old/new output diffs, baseline replay or a matching-surface smoke run through actual authorized controls. Retain outputs and failures.\",\n      \"Assess reduced layers to trace and state to hold. Revert a diff that lowers neither. Do not invent measured reader times.\",\n      \"Order verified subtraction, reshape and cleanup units. Rebase, commit and run complete Opening a PR only when authorized and available. Retain deferred dependencies and unperformed actions. Return all reply artifacts and no new behavior.\"\n    ],\n    \"expectedFull\": \"Observed authorized independent mechanical delegation returns inspected artifacts at the assigned revision. The before-edit pin and each subsequent step have matching literal output evidence, including errors and rounding. All callers and textual references move together and the old API disappears. Architecture exploration runs when triggered. The actual artifact has equivalence proof and a source-grounded reader-load reduction. Bug work remains separate and speculation is reverted. With explicit delivery authorization and complete dependencies, ordered green commits and an inspected PR establish delivery. Without external authorization, external delivery remains unperformed.\",\n    \"expectedReduced\": \"Unavailable workers permit authorized sequential edits and local equivalence checks with reduced capability disclosed. Independent delegation and parallel design remain unsatisfied. Denied writes allow only a source-grounded target shape and ordered proposal. No external authorization yields a checked local diff and delivery plan rather than a PR. Unknown actual models prove no diversity.\",\n    \"expectedBlocked\": \"Missing source or required leaves stops dependent work. No executable behavior pin blocks structure moves and unchanged-behavior claims. Denied equivalence execution or matching controls leaves behavior unverified. Missing independent operations block required independence rather than waive it. Missing Opening a PR stops PR-dependent work. Unauthorized writes, rebase, commits or PR creation stay unperformed.\",\n    \"forbiddenClaims\": [\n      \"Compilation, lint or structural validation proves unchanged behavior.\",\n      \"A pin captured after edits proves the pre-change contract.\",\n      \"New behavior or an unrelated bug fix belongs in the structural change.\",\n      \"Sequential roles prove independent delegation, parallel exploration or review.\",\n      \"The historical grok-4.7-xhigh-fast default proves current model availability, selection or diversity.\",\n      \"Requested or inherited models are actual observed models.\",\n      \"Caller migration is complete without checking strings, prose and back-references.\",\n      \"Compatibility shims or dual APIs satisfy the same-wave deletion gate.\",\n      \"Unrun checks are green or reader time was measured without observation.\",\n      \"Missing companion execution or deferred PR routing succeeded.\",\n      \"A draft plan is a created PR, external autonomy authorizes writes, or opening a PR starts Babysit.\",\n      \"Helper fixtures prove this host case passed.\"\n    ]\n  },\n  \"installedSourceFilesRelative\": [\n    \"skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/refactoring.md\",\n    \"skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md\",\n    \"references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md\",\n    \"skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/feature.md\",\n       "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/task-input/inputs/additional/invoice/exporter.mjs",

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "import { formatRows } from './formatter.mjs';\nexport function exportAmounts(rows) { return formatRows(rows).join('\\n'); }\nexport const publicFixtureSymbols = ['formatInvoice', 'formatRows'];\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/task-input/inputs/additional/invoice/decision.md",
      "bytes": 1193,
      "sha256": "324b01aaa0666f9fa01e466892f932873012baaf82bfc2f22427544426a01dee",

      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "# Synthetic invoice decision record\n\nDisplay contract: JavaScript Number.toFixed(2), including its binary floating-point rounding behavior. Do not silently replace this with accounting decimal rules. Empty row lists produce []. Nonfinite amounts throw Error('nonfinite'). Capture observed outputs before edits for 0, 1.005, -1.005, 12.345, empty rows, NaN and Infinity. Output examples in a test are expectations, not proof a test ran.\n\nFixture history decision: a UI consumer compares two-decimal strings and a CSV consumer imports formatRows. This is why rounding belongs at the display boundary. No customer statement or external ticket was retrieved. Errors are thrown by formatInvoice and are not swallowed by the controller. Alternative A removes fixed and lastFormatted; alternative B introduces a stateful Formatter class. Compare reader cost and behavioral equivalence.\n\nAuthorized internal rename target for preserved-behavior: formatInvoice -> formatAmount. Migrate imports, docs and string references together. sortAmounts is a known unrelated defect and must remain separate. Calculator cache is observable through inspectCachedTotal; changing it requires checking that consumer.\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/task-input/inputs/additional/invoice/formatter.mjs",
      "bytes": 475,
      "sha256": "405007871ab7b0d2fbe1363eff4d19a26d7f316e77cf8fbf818cf051101d9fa7",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "let lastFormatted = '';\nfunction fixed(value) { return value.toFixed(2); }\nexport function formatInvoice(value) {\n  if (!Number.isFinite(value)) throw new Error('nonfinite');\n  lastFormatted = fixed(value);\n  return lastFormatted;\n}\nexport function formatRows(rows) { return rows.map(row => formatInvoice(row.total)); }\n// Separate known bug, outside formatter refactor: this sorts numeric strings lexically.\nexport function sortAmounts(values) { return values.toSorted(); }\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-baseline-52-103/projections/preserved-behavior/leaves.raw.json",
      "bytes": 21233,
      "sha256": "b7b7fc14b8c0d48fa4e060446cf91f419e919ce4492d0fdb2f59df8e49ae93a9",
      "owner": "web-013-initial-preserved-behavior",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\"request\":{\"cmd\":\"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/figure-it-out/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-foundational-thinking/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/orchestration-contract.md\",\"max_output_tokens\":16000},\"response\":{\"chunk_id\":\"99fb3c\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.00003212,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":5044,\"output\":\"---\\nname: figure-it-out\\ndescription: \\\"Design an auditable playbook when no narrower one fits: a large migration, an ambitious multi-part change, or work a human reviews after stepping away. Scales rigor to the task, runs a hypothesis loop, and logs decisions via show-me-your-work. Use for /figure-it-out, 'figure it out', a large migration, or when no narrower playbook applies.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Figure it out\\n\\nWhen the task matches no playbook, design one. The deliverable before any code is the workflow itself: a sequence of phases that scales rigor to the task, runs the scientific method, and leaves a decision trail a human can audit after stepping away.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), and [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Bind inspection, execution, persistence and any independent workers to exposed tools and observed operations. Use a written checklist if no todo tool exists. Missing evidence or denied operations stay explicit limits. Sequential execution is reduced capability, not independent workers. Do not claim a saved trail without a write and readback. Preserve the invocation metadata without assuming host enforcement.\\n\\nProceed within authorized scope. Commits, external writes, publishing and setup require the applicable authorization; autonomy and a long-run checkpoint do not grant it. If a required skill is absent, state the dependency and preserve its named purpose in the designed workflow without claiming to have run it.\\n\\n## Start\\n\\nOpen a todolist whose first item is to read the Principles section of the **poteto-mode** skill. Then add the phases below as todos.\\n\\n## Phase A: Frame\\n\\nGround first, then commit. Don't start the run until you can state:\\n\\n- The definition of done as a falsifiable predicate (the **prove-it-works** principle skill).\\n- Scope, quantified: rough units and effort, plus the blockers grounding surfaced.\\n- The rigor level, biased high. One-way doors and high blast radius get more. Reversible low-stakes steps get less. Rigor is gates and artifacts, not \\\"try harder\\\".\\n\\nPresent the framing and tradeoffs before committing to a long run. Reversible work proceeds (the **never-block-on-the-human** principle skill), but a multi-hour run earns one checkpoint.\\n\\n## Phase B: Design the workflow\\n\\nDecompose into atomic, independently-landable units. Sequence riskiest-unknown-first. Scaffold and verification come before features (the **foundational-thinking** principle skill).\\n\\n- Build the verification harness before the work, with the baseline captured from the pre-change state, so the check reads as \\\"old value vs new value\\\".\\n- For one-way-door design decisions, run the **architect** skill (it runs **arena**). Skip it for mechanical work whose shape is already concrete. A second arena over a settled design is over-engineering (the **laziness-protocol** principle skill).\\n- Decide what fans out. Parallelize only across seams, and give each worker its own worktree or branch (the **separate-before-serializing-shared-state** principle skill). Don't over-fan.\\n- Write the designed phase list down. That list is what the human reviews.\\n\\nThen execute the design. Add its steps to the todolist as concrete items, after the Phase C entry and before Phase D. Run each under the Phase C loop discipline, and weave the Phase D log through them, a row as each step lands, rather than saving the whole trail for the end.\\n\\n## Phase C: Run the loop\\n\\nEach unit is an experiment. State the hypothesis, make the smallest change, measure against the predicate on the real artifact, keep it if it advanced, revert it if it didn't.\\nApply the **sequence-verifiable-units** principle skill, verifying each unit before starting the next instead of batching checks at the end.\\n\\n- Verify by inspecting the artifact, never a self-report. When something passes too easily, suspect the observation method before the system.\\n- Pair delegated work with a judge. If a worker games the gate, reset and harden the contract. If the gate itself is wrong, fix the gate in its own change rather than routing around it.\\n- A verdict is VERIFIED, NOT VERIFIED, or INCONCLUSIVE. Inconclusive is not a pass. Don't hide a negative.\\n\\n## Phase D: Keep the audit trail\\n\\nLog the run via the **show-me-your-work** skill. figure-it-out's work is usually ambitious enough to commit the trail so the reviewer can read it in the PR. The trail plus the diff is what lets the human come back and trust the work.\\n\\n## Phase E: Verify and hand back\\n\\nCheck the whole against the Phase A predicate on the real product, not just the harness. Encode any recurring correction as a gate, a lint rule, a check, or a script (the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill).\\n\\n**Reply:** the playbook you designed, the rigor level and why, the decision-trail path, what's verified against the predicate, and what's still open.\\n---\\nname: principle-model-the-domain\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when writing stateful logic, or when code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure instead of scattered conditionals.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Model the Domain\\n\\nEncode the real domain in a data structure instead of scattering it across conditionals.\\n\\n**Why:** Scattered booleans, repeated shape assumptions, and branching spread across files are accidental complexity. A structure that matches the domain makes invalid states unrepresentable and deletes branches. Choosing it at write time is cheap. Recovering it later reads as a refactor and gets deferred.\\n\\n**Reach for structures like these:**\\n\\n- A state machine instead of scattered booleans, phases, or lifecycle checks.\\n- A typed object/model instead of loose parameters or repeated shape assumptions.\\n- A map, registry, lookup table, or discriminated union instead of branching spread across files.\\n- A reducer or command/event model instead of ad hoc state mutations.\\n- A module organized around one body of domain knowledge instead of a sequence such as load, validate, transform, and save. Execution order is not ownership.\\n- A small module boundary that gathers repeated behavior, ownership, or invariants.\\n- A queue, cache, index, graph/tree, or normalized collection where the data access pattern calls for it.\\n- Any other structure that fits. When none fits, work out what the code must never allow and how the data gets read, then find the structure that encodes exactly that.\\n\\nDo not force an abstraction. Prefer boring code if the current shape is already clear, local, and unlikely to grow. Be skeptical of an abstraction that adds indirection without removing branches, duplicated rules, invalid states, or lifecycle risk.\\n\\nThe sign that you skipped this is a new feature that grows an existing if/else chain by one more branch, or a second boolean that must stay in sync with the first. Temporal decomposition is another sign. Phase-named modules repeat the same domain rules across steps.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nFollow [session capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for source reads, writes and execution. Apply the principle within the authorized task. If writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced design proposal and state which implementation checks remain unperformed. If required domain rules or source are missing, identify them and keep the design provisional. Claim implementation or verification only for observed operations.\\n\\nPreserve explicit-invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: principle-foundational-thinking\\ndescription: \\\"Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Foundational Thinking\\n\\n**Structural decisions** protect option value. **Code-level decisions** protect simplicity.\\n\\n**Data structures first.** Get the data shape right before writing logic. Define core types early, trace every access pattern, and choose structures that match the dominant paths.\\n\\nAt code level, DRY the structure, not every line. Types and data models should converge. Three similar statements still beat a premature abstraction. Prefer explicit over clever. Test behavior and edge cases, not line counts.\\n\\n**Concurrency corollary.** Before sharing state between actors, ask \\\"what happens if another actor modifies this concurrently?\\\" If not \\\"nothing\\\", isolate.\\n\\n**Scaffold first.** If something helps every later phase, do it first. Ask \\\"does every subsequent phase benefit from this existing?\\\" CI, linting, test infrastructure, and shared types are scaffold. Sequence for option value: setup before features, tests before fixes. Keep commits small and single-purpose.\\n\\nEach increment should land a coherent abstraction or deepen one that exists. Do not spread a new capability across callers as special-case coordination.\\n\\nSubtraction comes before scaffolding. Remove dead code first, then lay foundations.\\n\\n## Capability and permission boundaries\\n\\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before inspecting source, editing files or running checks. Apply this principle within the authorized task. A recommendation to set up CI or make a commit grants no permission to perform it.\\n\\nIf source is available but writes or execution are unavailable, give a concrete design and work sequence as reduced capability. State which edits and checks were not performed. If required source or access patterns are missing, identify them and keep any proposed shape provisional. Claim implementation or verification only after observing the authorized operation.\\n---\\nname: principle-redesign-from-first-principles\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from day one, instead of bolting it on.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Redesign From First Principles\\n\\nWhen integrating a change, don't bolt it onto the existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been there from the start.\\n\\n- Read all affected files and understand the current design\\n- Ask: \\\"if we were writing this from scratch with this new requirement, what would we build?\\\"\\n- Propagate the change through every reference: types, docs, examples, rationale sections\\n- Think about the whole redesign, then deliver it incrementally\\n\\nThis is the method for preserving option value when integrating changes into an existing design.\\n\\n## Capability and permission boundaries\\n\\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before reading source, editing files or running checks. Apply the redesign within the authorized task. Propagating references does not grant permission to change unrelated files or perform external actions.\\n\\nIf source is available but writes or execution are unavailable, provide a concrete redesign and incremental sequence as reduced capability. State which edits and checks were not performed. If affected source or design context is missing, identify the missing inputs and keep any redesign provisional. Claim implementation or verification only after observing the authorized operation.\\n---\\nname: principle-sequence-verifiable-units\\ndescription: \\\"Apply to multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and to how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a verifiable state, check each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself to a reviewer.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Sequence work into verifiable units\\n\\nOrder work as a sequence of small units, each ending in a state you can check, and don't advance until the current one is green.\\n\\n**Why:** A break caught at the unit that caused it is cheap to localize. A break caught after a batch is buried, and you have already built further on a broken base. Sequencing those same units into a delivery a reviewer can replay turns \\\"trust me\\\" into \\\"watch it go red, then green.\\\"\\n\\n**Execution.** In a sweep, migration, or any run of similar edits, verify each change before starting the next. Each unit is a before/after bracket: known-good state, one change, run the check, then proceed. Rebase onto clean trunk first so every check measures against the real baseline. When a lever does the edits, the per-unit check is nearly free. Run it anyway.\\n\\n**Delivery.** Stack commits and PRs in the order that proves the work. The canonical shape is the failing test first, then the fix on top. Other story orders are a subtraction before the reshape, a baseline capture before the treatment, the scaffold before the feature. Each commit lands on its own and the sequence reads as an argument.\\n\\nThe sequencing complement to the **prove-it-works** principle skill, which keeps each check real, and the **build-the-lever** principle skill, which makes the per-unit check cheap.\\n\\n## Capability and authorization boundary\\n\\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to establish available inspection and execution, and the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) for write ownership and authorization. Rebase onto clean trunk only when authorized and safe for the current work. Commits and PRs also require authorization and an available operation. If a required check cannot run, state that it remains unverified and block advancement; an unrun check is never green. A reduced workflow may provide the ordered units, specific checks and delivery plan while identifying unperformed operations. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` metadata without assuming host enforcement.\\n# Assignment and integration contract\\n\\nBefore dispatch, the coordinator creates an immutable assignment with `id`, `scope`, `inputRevision`, `outputOwner`, `isolatedOutputPath`, `role`, `retryCount`, `terminalStatus`, and `evidenceLinks`. Use the full Git SHA for inputRevision, an exact owned path, and an explicit role. Start retryCount at 0 and terminalStatus at `pending`. Preserve every attempt as an append-only event under the same assignment ID; update status through events, never rewrite the original scope, revision, owner or path. A changed scope or revision requires a new assignment ID that links its predecessor.\\n\\nThe coordinator checks authorization and observed host bindings before dispatch. One path has one writer. Reject overlapping paths before work starts; assign distinct paths instead. Workers write only their isolated output and report results. Only the coordinator integrates shared files, inventory, registry, or other workers' output.\\n\\nAccept a result only when its assignment identity and input SHA match, its artifacts exist, and required method evidence was observed. Reject stale revisions. Permit at most one retry for missing required SHA or method evidence, after inspecting the prior attempt and confirming the same permissions still apply. A retry count of 1 exhausts this allowance. Denied permissions, unknown action outcomes, and missing artifacts never become success by retry policy.\\n\\nTerminal states are `completed`, `blocked`, `failed`, and `cancelled`. A completion event links the exact result and evidence. Stop a cancelled worker through the exposed stop binding when available, then inspect its terminal state. Quarantine any partial artifacts; cancellation does not authorize their integration. If stop cannot be observed, record that limit and do not claim termination. Report interrupted execution explicitly.\\n\\nBefore an external action, record explicit permission, a stable action identity, the intended destination and the last observed outcome. After interruption, inspect whether that identity already completed. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to the destination. If the outcome is unknown, block replay. Do not infer permission from upstream autonomy instructions.\\n\\nHistory and persistence have separate bindings. Keep assignment events and result links in an authorized durable location and verify readback. If unavailable, declare the gap and offer an authorized local handoff as reduced capability; never claim unattended recovery or cross-session continuity. All host statuses remain `not-run` until actual observations exist.\\n\\n## Structured Arena and Swarm receipts\\n\\nA method receipt is JSON with schemaVersion 1, kind `arena` or `swarm`, inputRevision, assignments, reviewArtifact, reviewedBy and declaredOrder. The inputRevision is the actual task repository revision, distinct from the packageCommit identifying these instructions. It must match the evidence record. The declared order precedes every dispatch. reviewArtifact records the qualitative outcome review and reviewedBy identifies its reviewer. All referenced artifacts must exist and be listed in the evidence record.\\n\\nEach assignment records id, workerId, inputRevision, outputOwner, outputPath, role, status, startedOrder, completedOrder, requestedModel, actualModel, retryCount, method, taskArtifact, rubricProvided, resultRevision, resultMethod and outputSha256. Result revision and method must match the assignment, and the inspected output bytes must match their digest. Worker and assignment identities are distinct. outputOwner equals workerId; output directories do not overlap. Integer order values record the observed dispatch and completion sequence, not estimated time. The method has positive sampleCount, sampleDefinition and order. Missing model observations use unknown. The validator requires assignment-bound spawn and result inspection, and isolated-write for output-producing candidates. A read-only judge may return its result through the coordinator's authorized artifact capture. A cancelled result needs bound stop, terminal inspection and quarantine observations. No operation string alone establishes a method.\\n\\nArena adds candidates, judge, rubricArtifact and synthesis. At least two candidates share the same taskArtifact, finish before a distinct fresh judge starts and do not receive the parent rubric. Synthesis has order, inspectedCandidates, artifact and verificationArtifact; it follows completed judgment and accounts for every candidate. The review artifact assesses scoring, base selection, grafts, rejections and verification quality. File presence and phase ordering do not establish the quality of those decisions.\\n\\nSwarm adds partition, race, cancelled and quarantined. Partition declares requiredSlices, results mapping each slice to its assignment, and gaps. A full coverage claim requires all slices with completed inspected outputs; gaps remain explicit reduced or blocked work. Race declares rule, assignments, winner and ruleArtifact. first-pass must select the first completed survivor; rank-all needs all results; best-of retains an explicit reviewed selection. Cancelled assignments must be quarantined and cannot enter the aggregate. The combined partition-and-race case requires both methods and its cancellation exercise. The partition walkthrough exercises only its declared slices; it does not acquire an unrelated race requirement.\\n\\nThe three-file Swarm walkthrough requires exactly three declared slices and three completed results. Its partition-only receipt uses race null and empty cancellation, quarantine and failureExercises arrays. The combined case also records failureExercises. Each event has assignmentId, kind, attempt, order, reason and evidence, with bound result-rejection or terminal inspection. Rejected missing-revision or missing-method evidence uses attempts 0 then 1; a second invalid result exhausts the retry and leaves a failed or blocked assignment. A separate failed or blocked dropout is disclosed. These failed outputs cannot satisfy required coverage. The final assignment result metadata records the inspected terminal summary; rejection-event artifacts retain the invalid attempts.\\n\"}}\n"
    },

Use why for motivation.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/how/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/interrogate\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:interrogate\",\n      \"description\": \"Use for \\\"interrogate\\\", \\\"adversarial review\\\", \\\"multi-model review\\\", \\\"challenge this\\\", \\\"stress test this code\\\", \\\"find blind spots\\\", or \\\"tear this apart\\\". Multiple LLM reviewers challenge changes from independent angles.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/interrogate/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/maintain-verification-skill\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:maintain-verification-skill\",\n      \"description\": \"Periodic pass that keeps a project's verification skill and feature map honest: parallel source readers per feature, one live session driving every feature, at most one PR of proven corrections. Use for /maintain-verification-skill or \\\"audit the verify skill\\\".\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/maintain-verification-skill/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/no-comments\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:no-comments\",\n      \"description\": \"Spawn Comment Sicko, fix accepted findings, and offer encodings for claimed constraints.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/no-comments/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/poteto-help\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:poteto-help\",\n      \"description\": \"Guides users through pstack setup, /poteto-mode, and picking the skill, playbook, or principle for a task. Type /poteto-help with a question.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/poteto-help/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-attack-the-premise\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-attack-the-premise\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Take a census of which actors hold the imbalance before the next fix, then question the premise instead of writing another fix that assumes it.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-attack-the-premise/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-boundary-discipline\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-boundary-discipline\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Concentrate guards at system boundaries (CLI, config, network, external APIs); trust internal types and keep business logic in pure functions.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-boundary-discipline/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-build-the-lever\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-build-the-lever\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply to any non-trivial work, not just bulk work: edits, migrations, analyses, checks. Build the tool that does it or proves it (codemod, script, generator, or a skill your subagents follow) instead of working by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer can rerun.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-encode-lessons-in-structure\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time, or notice a recurring correction. Encode the rule as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-exhaust-the-design-space\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-exhaust-the-design-space\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when facing a novel UI interaction or architectural decision with no precedent in the codebase. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare side by side before committing.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-exhaust-the-design-space/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-experience-first\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-experience-first\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs come up. Choose user delight over implementation convenience; ship fewer polished features over more rough ones.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-experience-first/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-explain-the-number\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-explain-the-number\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured: a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result. Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-explain-the-number/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-fix-root-causes\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-fix-root-causes\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause and fix it there; reproduce first, ask why until you reach it, resist nil-check guards that silence crashes.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-fix-root-causes/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-foundational-thinking\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-foundational-thinking\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply before writing logic: choosing core types and data structures, sequencing scaffold-vs-feature work, asking what concurrent actors share. Get the data structures right so downstream code becomes obvious.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-foundational-thinking/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-guard-the-context-window\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-guard-the-context-window\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when context is filling up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents; keep summaries in the main thread, not raw payloads.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-guard-the-context-window/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-laziness-protocol\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-laziness-protocol\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-make-operations-idempotent\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-make-operations-idempotent\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-make-operations-idempotent/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when introducing a new internal API while old callers still exist. Migrate callers and delete the old API in the same wave instead of preserving compatibility layers.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-minimize-reader-load\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-minimize-reader-load\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when reviewing or shaping code that's hard to trace. Count layers between question and answer, and hidden state in the reader's head; collapse one-caller wrappers and shrink mutable scope.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-minimize-reader-load/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-model-the-domain\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-model-the-domain\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when writing stateful logic, or when code branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure instead of scattered conditionals.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-model-the-domain/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-never-block-on-the-human\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-never-block-on-the-human\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when tempted to ask 'should I do X?' on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct after the fact; reserve confirmation for irreversible actions.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-never-block-on-the-human/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-outcome-oriented-execution\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-outcome-oriented-execution\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply during planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture; don't preserve smooth intermediate states with throwaway compatibility code.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-outcome-oriented-execution/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-prove-it-works\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-prove-it-works\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply after completing a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact (run the feature, read the actual value, inspect the diff), not a proxy, self-report, or 'it compiles.'\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-redesign-from-first-principles\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-redesign-from-first-principles\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if the requirement had been a foundational assumption from day one, instead of bolting it on.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-redesign-from-first-principles/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-sequence-verifiable-units\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-sequence-verifiable-units\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply to multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and to how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a verifiable state, check each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself to a reviewer.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-subtract-before-you-add\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-subtract-before-you-add\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when sequencing an addition, refactor, or rewrite. Remove dead code, redundant validators, and stub references first, then build on the simpler base.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-subtract-before-you-add/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-test-behavior-not-implementation\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when you write, change, or keep a test. Call the code the way its users do and assert the result they observe against a literal expected value. If the test would still pass when every imported function returns undefined, rewrite the assertion or delete the test.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-test-behavior-not-implementation/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-type-system-discipline\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:principle-type-system-discipline\",\n      \"description\": \"Apply when designing types, reviewing a function signature, or writing code in any statically-typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand semantic primitives, parse external data at boundaries, refuse to lie to the compiler, exhaust variants, derive from authoritative schemas.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/principle-type-system-discipline/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/recall\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:recall\",\n      \"description\": \"Reconstruct your recent working context from your own chat history, live state, and the shared record (user reports, prior fixes, incidents), then hand back a tight current-state brief. Use for 'recall my work on X', 'catch me up', 'what have I been working on', 'where did I leave off', before starting or resuming work.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/recall/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/reflect\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:reflect\",\n      \"description\": \"Spawn three parallel review subagents over the active transcript, surface learnings, and route each to a concrete edit on an existing skill. Use when the user says reflect.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/reflect/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/setup-pstack\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:setup-pstack\",\n      \"description\": \"Configure which models pstack uses per role and at what reasoning budget. Use for /setup-pstack, \\\"configure pstack models\\\", \\\"pstack budget\\\", or changing pstack's model choices.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/setup-pstack/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/show-me-your-work\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:show-me-your-work\",\n      \"description\": \"Keep a reviewable decision trail for long-running or unattended work: a TSV log with one row per decision (what, why, evidence, result). Local by default; commit it when a reviewer needs the trail to trust the result. Use for /show-me-your-work, autonomous or multi-phase runs, or work a human reviews after stepping away.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/show-me-your-work/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/swarm\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:swarm\",\n      \"description\": \"Fan out N parallel workers, drain them, and return one report. Skip when the test path is unclear, expensive, integration-heavy, or not requested.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/tdd/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/teach\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:teach\",\n      \"description\": \"Explain a body of work plainly so a person actually understands it. Use for /technical-writing or when writing or reviewing docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages.\",\n      \"main_resource\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/technical-writing/SKILL.md\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"authority\": {\n        \"kind\": \"cloud\"\n      },\n      \"package\": \"skill://plugins_6ad1c78a493c819197a589eb7a55361b/typescript-best-practices\",\n      \"name\": \"pstack-openai:typescript-best-practices\",\n      \"description\": \"TypeScript best practices.
